Ethereum has gained roughly 11%, reclaiming its 200-day EMA band.

ETH could target $2,600 and $2,800 if bullish momentum continues.

Binanceâs Ethereum leverage ratio reached 0.162, its highest level in five months.

Ethereumâs latest rally looks encouraging. But the derivatives market is flashing a warning that traders shouldnât ignore. ETH has climbed back above its 200-day EMA band, recovering from below $2,200 in late October to approximately $2,430, marking an 11% rise.

The recovery puts higher price targets back on the table. However, rising leverage could make the next move considerably more volatile.

With Ethereum back above its 200-day EMA, bulls have an opportunity to extend the recovery. A sustained advance could push ETH toward $2,600, with $2,800 emerging as another potential target.

Still, the $2,200 level has become crucial. If ETH fails to defend it and turns the former support into resistance, the recovery could quickly lose steam. A breakdown could expose $2,000 or even $1,800.

Onchain data adds another complication. Ethereumâs Estimated Leverage Ratio on Binance has climbed to approximately 0.1862, its highest reading in five months.

The indicator measures open futures positions relative to ETH reserves held on the exchange. Its rise suggests traders are using more leverage and taking on greater derivatives exposure.

Thatâs not automatically bullish. The ratio doesnât reveal whether positions are predominantly long or short, so treating it as confirmation of further upside would be premature.

Higher leverage makes the market more sensitive to sudden price movements. If ETH moves against crowded positions, liquidations could accelerate volatility and undermine the recovery.

For now, Ethereum price has reclaimed 200-day EMA offers a constructive technical signal, but the $2,200 defense remains essential. Bulls need to protect that level while managing the risks highlighted by Binanceâs rising leverage ratio.Â

#5226. 99% Cold Wallet Rule Mulled by South Korea Following Binance Hack

South Korean financial regulators are reportedly considering introducing much stricter cryptocurrency storage requirements for local exchanges following the massive $6.3 billion Binance hack.

According to a Nov. 3 report by South Korean news outlet News1, the country’s Financial Services Commission (FSC) is discussing raising the minimum proportion of customer cryptocurrencies that exchanges must store in cold wallets from the current 80% to approximately 99%.

The potential regulatory change comes just days after Binance, the world’s largest cryptocurrency exchange, suffered one of the biggest security breaches in cryptocurrency history.

However, the proposed 99% threshold remains under discussion, and no official decision has been made.

Under South Korea’s existing Virtual Asset User Protection Act, cryptocurrency exchanges are required to store at least 80% of customers’ digital assets in cold wallets.

Unlike hot wallets, which are connected to the internet and used to process everyday transactions, cold wallets are kept offline, making them significantly less vulnerable to remote hacking attacks.

However, the proportion of assets stored offline varies significantly among South Korea’s leading cryptocurrency exchanges.

According to Financial Supervisory Service data cited by local media, Upbit currently has one of the highest cold wallet storage ratios in the country.

As of the end of September, the exchange held approximately 99.17% of customer assets in cold wallets.

Meanwhile, rival cryptocurrency exchange Bithumb stored approximately 94.76% of its assets offline, while Coinone’s cold wallet ratio stood at 85%.

Google Gemini AI predicts a move for Cardano (ADA) that would see it trade at nearly 40 times its current price. For this outlandish prediction to hit, Gemini AI believes a hypothetical landmark catalyst would need to land for Cardano, acknowledging just how unlikely the event is.

For context, ADA is trading below $0.189 on Tuesday (November 3), after losing a key support zone the previous day, and it has had a rough October, down -22.2%. This is a “what if” scenario, and it assumes the following.

(SOURCE: Google Gemini AI Predicts a Wild Cardano Rally)

Late in Q4, a consortium of major central banks and Tier-1 financial institutions announces a pilot for wholesale tokenized bond settlement.

After a bake-off, they pick Cardano as the settlement layer, citing built-in compliance that doesn’t depend on bolt-on smart contract logic.

Within days, a US government digital asset reserve discloses that it has quietly accumulated ADA as a settlement-layer strategic asset. Spot ADA ETF inflows go parabolic. Short sellers learn what the word “squeeze” means.

On the weekly chart, ADA has spent a long stretch trapped in a descending channel, with levels at $0.41, $0.78, $1.18, and eventually $3 in play if it breaks out.

In this scenario, the Zurich Accord headline blasts ADA through the $0.25 breakout zone on enormous volume, which flips the channel’s upper boundary from resistance to support.

Each of those historical supply zones then becomes a stepping stone instead of a ceiling: $0.41, then $0.78, then $1.18. Once price clears the $3.09–$3.10 all-time high, there’s no overhead resistance at all, which is the textbook setup for price discovery.

Momentum indicators go deeply overbought and stay overbought, as they famously do in parabolic phases. Fair warning: today’s actual chart is bearish, with ADA slipping out of the ascending channel that carried it for months. Bulls need to reclaim $0.23 before this fantasy even gets off the ground.

#5224. Ex-XRP Treasury Reveals Exit From $5 Million Stake in Ripple

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

A fresh annual report of British company VivoPower PLC, filed with the U.S. Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC) on November 2, 2026, officially disclosed the parameters of the closure of its cryptocurrency strategy.

The documents recorded the creation and subsequent liquidation of a $5 million stake in the private company Ripple Labs, while also making public the price of their buyback at $300 apiece.

According to the filing, in May 2025, VivoPower announced a large-scale treasury expansion with a target investment volume of up to $100 million in XRP tokens and Ripple Labs equity. In practice, the investments turned out to be more modest: the XRP treasury allocated $5.0 million to the direct acquisition of 41,810 Ripple Labs shares in January 2026.

For institutional investors, buying stakes in the fintech giant itself was a key way to make a large bet on the ecosystem's success.

However, the experiment lasted only nine months from its announcement. As early as February, VivoPower's board of directors decided to fully wind down the crypto direction. Management recognized holding digital assets as less profitable than developing artificial intelligence infrastructure and redirected capital to the purchase of a 41.5 MW data center in Mo i Rana, Norway, for $37.7 million.

The process of liquidating this $5 million stake brought the crypto market an important price insight. In April 2026, Ripple Labs conducted a targeted buyback, taking 882 shares from VivoPower at $300 apiece. The deal brought the British company $264,600 in cash and $159,122 in net profit, giving the community a rare and hard marker of how Ripple's management itself values its business in private trades.

A direct sale of the remaining part of the stake on the open market proved impossible due to the non-public status of the securities. As a result, VivoPower entered into agreements to sell the economic rights to the shares to two South Korean partners, KWeather Co. and Lean Ventures.

As of the filing date, the Korean deals have not yet been legally completed. The remaining 40,928 shares are locked in the Korea Securities Depository (KSD) under a strict one-year lock-up restriction. Due to this freeze and the lack of market liquidity, independent appraisers lowered the book value of this stake to $4.3 million (about $106.16 per share).

The VivoPower story clearly showed the crypto industry the flip side of institutional investments: a direct purchase of stakes in Ripple Labs comes with strict lock-ups and low liquidity, where the only fast source of fiat for exiting the ecosystem is the issuer itself through a private buyback procedure.

#5222. Coinbase gets New Jersey staking relief after three years — Details

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Coinbase has secured a significant legal victory in New Jersey after state regulators formally dropped their lawsuit against the cryptocurrency exchange over its staking services.

According to Molly Abraham, VP of Legal at Coinbase, the state has also lifted the cease-and-desist order previously imposed on Coinbase, allowing the platform to resume offering staking services to customers in New Jersey.

The dispute dates back to 2023, when New Jersey’s Bureau of Securities took action against Coinbase. It alleged that its staking program constituted an unregistered securities offering.

Regulators argued that Coinbase had failed to register its staking-as-a-service program under state securities laws. As a result, the exchange faced restrictions that prevented eligible New Jersey residents from participating in its staking program and earning rewards on their digital assets.

With the lawsuit now dismissed and the enforcement order lifted, Coinbase can restore staking access for its New Jersey customers.

As expected, Coinbase’s Chief Policy Officer, Faryar Shirzad, welcomed the decision, describing it as an important development for staking access across the U.S.

However, the company also indicated that its regulatory battle is far from over. Wisconsin, Maryland, California, and Washington remain among the states where staking-related restrictions or disputes need to be addressed.

Coinbase has witnessed growing traction in tokenized stocks lately, with it ranked second among tokenized stock issuers by decentralized exchange (DEX) trading volume over the past seven days.

In fact, according to Token Terminal, its tokenized assets now account for 26.7% of the total trading volume, narrowly trailing Robinhood’s 27.3%. Source: Token Terminal

However, broader market indicators point towards weakening investor demand. For instance, the Coinbase Premium Index has remained negative for 39 consecutive days. This indicated that Bitcoin [BTC] has consistently traded at a discount on Coinbase compared to global cryptocurrency markets.

On 30th October, the index dropped to -0.2682%, reflecting heightened selling pressure and weaker buying interest following the Binance hack. In fact, at press time too, it remained negative at -0.1847%. This suggested that selling pressure had not completely subsided.

Meanwhile, $COIN stock has been struggling too. It was changing hands at $137.83 at press time after a drop of 3.98%.

Morgan Stanley recently downgraded COIN from Equalweight to Underweight and slashed its price target from $258 to $120. Analyst Michael Cyprys cited deteriorating confidence in the company’s earnings recovery, weaker trading activity, a softer revenue outlook, and uncertainty surrounding the crypto market.

The investment bank also reduced its 2027 revenue and adjusted EBITDA forecasts by 28% and 47%, respectively.

All of this further coincided with the exchange reporting net income of $719 million for Q3 2026, compared to $433 million during the same period last year.

This means that despite New Jersey’s victory and its growing presence in tokenized stocks, there are significant headwinds still surrounding Coinbase’s business outlook.

#5221. Cboe and Citadel Sue to Stop US Stock Perps Hours After the CFTC Approved Them: Why Hyperliquid’s $5 Billion-a-Day Market Has the Most at Stake

Cboe and Citadel want US stock perps blocked. Hyperliquid already trades $5B of them a day, and ICE wants to bring it onshore.

A group backed by Cboe and Citadel Securities sued the CFTC on Tuesday, November 3, to block stock perps hours after the regulator cleared them for US exchanges, a fight that could decide whether Hyperliquid’s $5 billion-a-day market reaches American traders. The approval named no offshore venue, but it was aimed at one.

CFTC Chairman Michael Selig said Americans should not be “forced to turn to offshore, and often unregulated, venues” to trade perpetual futures on individual stocks.

🇺🇸 A CBOE AND CITADEL SECURITIES-BACKED GROUP SUES THE CFTC TO BLOCK PERPETUAL FUTURES ON US STOCKS, THE SAME DAY THE CFTC APPROVED THEM pic.twitter.com/38PNXGcM5f

— The Wolf Of All Streets (@scottmelker) November 3, 2026

The biggest of those venues runs on Hyperliquid. Trade.xyz, its largest builder market, did $4.99 billion in volume over the past 24 hours across 142 contracts on stocks, indexes and commodities, with $4.42 billion in open interest.

Six days ago, NYSE owner ICE agreed to bring those markets to US traders through a CFTC-regulated exchange of its own. HYPE jumped as much as 21% that day, to $97.74, just short of its $97.96 record.

The lawsuit didn’t knock it over. HYPE dipped to $81.01 early Wednesday and was back at $85.93 by 08:00 UTC, up 2.8% in 24 hours.

So what is the lawsuit about, and what does it mean for perp DEXs like Hyperliquid?

Market Intelligence: Crypto Analyst Predicts Best Low-Cap Crypto

Who gets to regulate single-stock perpetuals?  The Capital Markets Alliance, whose members include Cboe, Citadel Securities and the Healthy Markets Association, filed in federal court in Manhattan. It argues the contracts are security-based swaps, which belong to the SEC and are kept away from retail investors.

“This issue, however, is about investor protection for securities trading and the rule of law,” Cboe CEO Craig Donohue said.

The CFTC hit back. “Our markets should be governed by law, not litigation campaigns intended to block innovation,” a spokesperson said.

Four exchanges are waiting on the outcome. Kalshi filed first, on September 18, followed by Coinbase Derivatives, Bitnomial and Crypto.com’s North American Derivatives Exchange. CME Group is already suing the CFTC over Kalshi’s Bitcoin perpetual, approved in May.

DISCOVER: Ripple Prime Opens 24/7 RWA Perpetual Swaps: Institutional Capital Hits Tokenized Stocks and Commodities

Big enough to rival its crypto markets. Hyperliquid’s own crypto perpetuals did $7.14 billion over the same 24 hours, so trade.xyz’s markets made up about 41% of everything traded on the chain.

Indexes and oil lead, but single stocks fill most of the list:

Trade.xyz launched in October 2025 as the first market built on HIP-3, Hyperliquid’s system that lets outside teams list their own contracts. It now handles about 98% of HIP-3 volume and says it has processed $650 billion in total trading.

It is built for traders outside the US. Hyperliquid blocks US users on its own front end, and that is the gap Selig wants US exchanges to fill.

Very little for its markets today, and a lot for its path into the US. The case can’t touch trades on Hyperliquid itself. What it decides is whether American exchanges can compete for the same flow, and whether ICE can bring trade.xyz onshore.

The two outcomes cut in opposite directions:

Neither will happen soon. ICE’s exchange still needs its own CFTC approval, and neither company has given a launch date.

The first real test comes sooner. The court still has to decide whether to freeze the approval while the case runs. A freeze would stall US listings before Kalshi or Coinbase trade a single contract.

EXPLORE: HYPE Jumps +23% as CFTC Pathway Raises Hyperliquid US Market Hopes

HYPE has given back most of last week’s ICE spike. It hit $97.74 on October 29, slid back over the next few days, and now trades at $85.93, just above its 20-day EMA of $84.02.

For now, the fight over stock perps is about who can sell them to Americans. Hyperliquid already sells them to everyone else, $5 billion a day of it.

#5220. Tesla, Amazon Deal Fresh Blow to OKX Tokenization Push

OKX has suffered another big blow to its grand aspirations of tokenizing stocks after Tesla, Amazon, and four other big names turned down plans for the tokenization of their shares on the cryptocurrency exchange’s joint venture with New York Stock Exchange owner Intercontinental Exchange (ICE).

Six new companies have raised objections to their shares being tokenized on the OKXICE platform.

Among the new objectors are Elon Musk’s Tesla and SpaceX, e-commerce giant Amazon, streaming giant Netflix, semiconductor equipment maker Lam Research and fintech firm SoFi Technologies.

The new objections bring the total number of companies that have objected to the initiative to 56, further damaging OKX’s push into the realm of traditional finance.

In particular, five of the Magnificent Seven tech companies have turned down the proposal to have their stocks tokenized on the OKXICE platform. These include Tesla, Amazon, Microsoft, Alphabet and Nvidia.

What makes this rejection even more noteworthy is that the total number of targeted companies involved in this OKXICE joint venture is relatively low.

Back in early October, OKX and ICE announced their plan to offer tokenized versions of 62 major companies’ stocks.

However, 49 out of those 62 companies have formally opposed the proposal. Hence, about 79% of the initially targeted companies have rejected the plan. The other seven objections came from companies not in the initial selection.

Some of the most famous names in American finance and tech, including JPMorgan Chase, Goldman Sachs, Walmart, Coca-Cola, Intel, Oracle, AMD, Nike and Costco, have also expressed their objections.

As of the Nov. 2 notice, only 13 out of the original selection had yet to raise a publicly disclosed objection.

These names include Apple, Coinbase, Robinhood, Strategy, Circle and Palantir.

However, the fact that they have not raised a formal objection doesn’t necessarily mean they approve of the tokenization plan.

It is worth noting that Nov. 3 marks the end of the 30-day objection period for the companies that received their initial notice on Oct. 4.

Nonetheless, with almost four-fifths of the initially targeted companies raising objections, OKXICE faces an uphill battle to get sufficient corporate involvement to make its tokenized stock market appealing to investors.

OKXICE has not set a definite launch date yet. It also has not disclosed the number of tokenized stocks it will offer when it starts trading.

#5219. Hyperliquid faces 22% correction threat as H&S pattern tests $76-neckline

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Bitwise CIO Matt Hougan recently highlighted the price performance of top crypto assets in 2026. Among the top 20 crypto assets, he found that the median is down by 15%, while the mean is up 14%.

Outliers like Hyperliquid [HYPE], NEAR Protocol [NEAR], or ZCash [ZEC] have comfortably outperformed the mean.

At the time of writing, Hyperliquid’s weekly trend was bullish. In recent weeks, the altcoin has failed to push to new highs past $98, and its dip to $75.17 suggested a retracement might be in play.

The bounce to $97 on Thursday, 29th October, was followed by a 14.19% setback in the six days since. The price spike was a result of the Intercontinental Exchange (ICE) announcement that it would partner with Hyperliquid to introduce a regulated perpetual futures market in the United States.

In fact, crypto market maker Cumberland warned that Hyperliquid’s order book is fully transparent, and trades can be tracked.

That’s not all either as popular crypto analyst Ali Martinez observed that a potential head and shoulders top pattern may be forming for HYPE. A 22% correction is possible if the pattern is confirmed, which would happen with a 12-hour session close below the $76-$78 neckline.

In that scenario, the next price targets would be $62 and $59.

Recent weeks’ price swings from $75 to $98-$99 have built up sizeable pools of liquidity to either side. Key liquidation clusters have been swept too, with the latest being the dense short liquidations at $95.5.

The next pocket of liquidity sat at $74. HYPE’s retracement argument reinforced the bearish price bias too.

It was pointed out previously that the altcoin has failed to push to new highs on the charts. The recent liquidity sweep of the $97-region and the subsequent correction strengthened the idea that the bulls may not be in control of the market.

Rather, sellers showed clear intent with the recent losses and could succeed in driving a price correction below $75.

As Ali Martinez suggested, a pullback to $59-$62 may be possible. It would still be a healthy retracement within the weekly bullish trend.

Writer by choice, CryptoCurrency Writer, and Researcher by chance. Currently, focusing on financial news and analysis, as well as cryptocurrency news and data. One may not call me a crypto âEnthusiastâ but trust me I'm getting there.

Ripple has had one of its busiest stretches in years. It won a national trust bank charter, launched new partnerships and talked up XRP throughout Ripple Swell. Yet XRP is still trading near $1.27. Billy Trajcevski, Head of Strategic Partnerships at UpTrade, says the reason has more to do with Rippleâs structure than with the news itself.

Many holders worry that RLUSD, Rippleâs dollar stablecoin, is taking attention away from XRP. Trajcevski rejected that idea.

âXRP is not a stablecoin. RLUSD is. Itâs really as simple as that,â Trajcevski said.

Trajcevski argued that if XRP competed with RLUSD as a dollar substitute, it would simply sit at $1 forever, which no holder wants. âIf XRP replaces the dollar, weâre all screwed because the price is going to remain flat,â Trajcevski said.

According to Trajcevski, the two assets have different jobs:

A business paying an international invoice in another currency needs a float of XRP to do it, Trajcevski said, and RLUSD alone cannot do that job.

Asked why the good news has not shown up in XRPâs price, Trajcevski pointed to a basic business reality. Ripple is a private company that answers to its shareholders, and shareholders want to see several sources of revenue, not a business that depends on one asset.

âIf your entire business model is based on the success of the XRP token, the board will not be happy, shareholders will not be happy,â Trajcevski said.

That is the message many XRP holders do not want to hear. Ripple will keep building use cases and demand for XRP because that is in its interest, Trajcevski said, but it will not stake its whole business on the token.

Trajcevski also described how Rippleâs public stance toward XRP has changed. During the SEC lawsuit, Ripple kept its distance from the token, as any company in its position would. Now that the case is resolved, Trajcevski said, the company has âcome full circle,â and open support for XRP is back.

The clearest result from Swell, in Trajcevskiâs view, is that XRP has the full backing of the Ripple team.

Trajcevski picked out one headline from Swell that drew little attention: BNY, the worldâs largest custodian, now offers custody for XRP.

BNY serves major institutions, not retail investors. If it is offering XRP custody, Trajcevski said, there is clear institutional demand for the token, or Ripple has made it clear that demand is coming. Trajcevski called it the most bullish news of the event, even though it looked the least important.

Trajcevski also pointed to a session with Bullish CEO Tom Farley. Bullish already uses RLUSD in some of its products, and according to Trajcevski, the exchange is now in active talks with Ripple about new ways to use XRP that do not exist yet.

#5217. Ripple CEO Brad Garlinghouse Breaks Silence on XRP vs. RLUSD Debate

Ripple CEO Brad Garlinghouse addressed the ongoing debate over the potential impact of Ripple’s RLUSD stablecoin on XRP’s future, asserting that both assets are complementary rather than competitive.

On Nov. 3, Brad Garlinghouse offered his view in response to a comprehensive X post from a notable member of the XRP Ledger community named Vet, who outlined the key differences between the two assets associated with Ripple.

“Well said, Vet. Complementary, not competitive,” the Ripple chief executive tweeted.

The brief statement comes amid pushback as some XRP holders are increasingly concerned that Ripple’s aggressive foray into the stablecoin market may negatively impact demand for XRP in the future.

As part of his viral post, Vet explained that the key difference between the two assets revolves around the purposes they serve on the XRP Ledger.

“RLUSD is optional. XRP isn’t,” the commentator explained.

As the XRP Ledger’s native currency, XRP is essential for the core functions of the network. First, every account must have an XRP reserve. Second, transaction fees are paid in XRP.

RLUSD, on the other hand, is a dollar-denominated stablecoin issued by Ripple's regulated stablecoin business. The key difference is that, unlike XRP, the value of RLUSD is supposed to remain stable at roughly $1.

Another key difference lies in control over the assets.

Ripple has the ability to freeze RLUSD under certain conditions, for example, to meet legal obligations. There is no central entity that can freeze XRP, the native cryptocurrency, on the ledger.

According to Vet, another key advantage of XRP is its privileged status on the XRP Ledger's DEX.

The XRP Ledger has an automatic bridging function. In this way, if it makes sense to trade between currencies through XRP for a better exchange rate, trades can be automatically routed through XRP.

This is why XRP is so valuable for liquidity providers and market makers as an intermediary asset.

On the other hand, RLUSD would be much more suitable in cases where price stability is important, for example, for payroll, invoicing, corporate payments, etc.

At the same time, some XRP investors are worried that one day RLUSD may effectively replace XRP for some payment use cases.

The crucial question is this: If you can send a dollar-denominated stablecoin directly, why would you need to use an intermediary like XRP?

It’s also worth noting that RLUSD isn’t exclusive to the XRP Ledger. In fact, RLUSD also works on Ethereum and other supported blockchains. So increasing demand for RLUSD doesn’t necessarily lead to more use of the XRP Ledger.

And even if we’re seeing more RLUSD transactions on the XRP Ledger, this doesn’t automatically mean that increased demand for XRP to pay transaction fees necessarily guarantees a significant price surge for the cryptocurrency.

It’s not the first time Garlinghouse has been forced to comment on Ripple's changing priorities.

During Ripple’s Swell 2026 conference in New York on Oct. 28, Ripple’s CEO directly addressed rumors that Ripple is becoming increasingly focused on RLUSD rather than XRP.

“We will never give up on XRP,” Garlinghouse said.

#5215. The growing role of crypto in online casinos

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Online gaming is no longer limited to playing a game through a browser or downloading an app. Players now have access to multiplayer platforms, mobile games, subscription services, and online casinos, each offering different ways to play and interact. In particular, online casinos have expanded their gaming libraries, offering users a plethora of table games and online slots, as well as live dealer sessions and casino originals. Payment methods on these platforms have also changed. Bank cards and digital wallets remain widely used, but cryptocurrency has become another option for online services that support digital asset payments.

Bitcoin, Ethereum, and stablecoins such as USDT are now accepted by a number of online casino operators, allowing players to use digital assets for deposits and, depending on the platform, withdrawals. For players interested in digital currency gaming, BetUS Casino offers an online destination to explore and play casino games with crypto. Cryptocurrency in this space also raises practical questions about how transactions are processed, which platforms support which assets, and what players should consider before choosing an online casino.

Better internet, mobile tech, and cloud services have changed how online gaming works. Faster connections make real-time multiplayer games easier to play, and cloud services help platforms update games, manage accounts, and let people play on different devices. Now, games can get new content and features without players needing to download a whole new version. Buying digital content is also common on many platforms. These changes have greatly influenced a player’s expectations, especially around easy access and account management.

Online casinos have grown with these changes. Many now offer hundreds or even thousands of games, including slots, table games, and live dealer options, all available on desktop and mobile. Operators also handle digital services like player accounts, game purchases, deposits, and withdrawals. This has created an online space where playing games and paying for them are closely linked.

Cryptocurrency is becoming more common for online payments, giving platforms another way to accept money from users who already have digital assets. Unlike card payments, which go through banks and payment networks, crypto payments move assets directly between blockchain addresses. The kind of cryptocurrency matters too. Bitcoin and Ethereum are well known, but their prices can change quickly, which might affect how much a deposit is worth if the funds stay in those assets. Stablecoins like USDT are different because they are meant to keep a value close to the US dollar. This makes them helpful for people who want to pay digitally without as much price risk, though they still have some risks of their own.

Making a crypto deposit involves more than simply entering a payment amount. The platform or its payment provider supplies a wallet address and specifies which blockchain network to use. Users need to follow these instructions carefully, as assets such as USDT are available across multiple networks that may not be compatible with one another. Once funds are sent, the transaction is verified and recorded on the relevant blockchain. The receiving platform may require a set number of network confirmations before crediting the account. Transaction fees and processing times also vary depending on the asset, network activity, and the platform’s own deposit procedures.

Cryptocurrency and online casinos are connected in more ways than just adding Bitcoin or USDT as payment options. Some casinos are built specifically for users with digital assets, so their account balances, deposits, and withdrawals are set up for crypto transactions. Others add crypto tokens to their existing payment systems, which already include bank cards and digital wallets. For players, these differences can change how casinos manage funds and which currencies are available. Also, not every casino treats crypto the same way. Some may let you deposit with a certain cryptocurrency but not withdraw with it.

Online casino users now have more ways to pay, but other things still set casinos apart. They still compete on the games they offer, the software they use, mobile access, withdrawal rules, and customer support. Accepting several cryptocurrencies might attract people who use digital assets, but that doesn’t tell potential players much about the casino’s overall quality. Payment terms, game selection, and withdrawal conditions are still important.

As more online casinos start accepting cryptocurrency, players now have more choices than ever. But payment methods are just one factor to think about. The selection of games can be very different from one casino to another. Most well-known casinos have slots, table games, and live dealer options, but the number of games and the companies that make them can vary. Some casinos even create their own games or offer exclusive titles you won’t find elsewhere. For example, BetUS Casino has over 700 games with more games in the pipeline awaiting their launch, so players have plenty to try.

Casinos also use promotions to bring in new players, offering things like deposit bonuses, free bets, and other rewards. BetUS, for example, has several types of bonuses. One is a 150% Sportsbook bonus, which offers up to $750 across a player’s first three deposits, with a $50 minimum deposit and a 10x rollover requirement in sports. These offers can look appealing, but the attached conditions matter as much as the advertised rewards. Players should also consider licensing, account security, withdrawal policies, and customer support when comparing platforms. Casino ratings and independent reviews can help narrow options, but players should consider them alongside these practical details rather than treating them as a guarantee of quality.

Crypto payments let players control how they move their money, but it also places greater responsibility on the person making the transaction. Anyone using a personal wallet should protect their private keys and recovery phrases, as losing access to them can mean losing access to their funds. It is equally important to check the receiving address and blockchain network before confirming a transfer. Crypto transactions generally cannot be reversed once confirmed, so sending funds to an incorrect address or through an unsupported network can make recovery difficult or impossible. Players should also account for network fees, which can change with demand, and confirmation times, which may affect when a casino credits a deposit or processes a withdrawal.

It’s just as important to check out the casino itself. Just because a casino accepts Bitcoin, Ethereum, or USDT doesn’t mean it’s licensed or allowed to operate everywhere. Players should check which regulatory authority issued its licence, whether that licence covers online gambling, and whether the operator is permitted to accept customers from their jurisdiction. They should also understand the platform’s withdrawal procedures, including any limits, verification requirements, processing conditions, or restrictions on cryptocurrency withdrawals, before depositing funds.

Founded in 1994, BetUS is an online betting operator with more than 30 years of experience in the industry. Licensed and regulated by the government of Curaçao, the platform serves more than one million clients and offers sports betting, casino gaming, and headline news wagering markets. Its services include betting odds, sports analysis, and customer support, with a focus on providing users with access to wagering opportunities across different categories.

Crypto gaming, sometimes called Web3 or blockchain gaming, means playing online games or using entertainment platforms that use cryptocurrencies or blockchain technology. This includes online casino games that accept digital currencies and games where players can own or trade digital items.

Players usually send cryptocurrency from their own wallet or an exchange to the casino’s deposit address. Once the transaction is confirmed on the blockchain, the casino adds the funds to the player’s account. Withdrawing works in much the same way.

Many online casinos accept cryptocurrencies like Bitcoin and Ethereum. Some also support Litecoin and stablecoins such as USDT. Since payment options vary, it’s a good idea to check which coins and networks each casino supports.

Crypto casinos can be safer if they are run by licensed and regulated companies. Players should check for clear licensing details on the site and confirm them with the authority that issued them. It’s also important to keep wallets secure and gamble responsibly.

The $0.84 level was a key ceiling and roadblock in Q3. If bulls defend it, a recovery may be feasible, but a strong uptrend will be only confirmed if price climbs above $0.95.

On the other hand, dropping below $0.84 would bring SUI back to its Q3 price range of $0.66-$0.84. In such a scenario, if market sentiment weakens in Q4, especially with a likely Fed rate hike in December, then SUI price may hit the lower bound of $0.66.

That would be a downside risk of 10% ($0.75) or 21% if the drop extends to the lower bound at $0.66. In other words, short sellers may still profit from SUI if market sentiment sours further in Q4.

On the liquidation maps, the upside liquidity pools (leveraged shorts) were between $0.91 and $0.97, aligning with the red zone marked on the price charts. A liquidity hunt may push the price to this area, but a decisive rebound would be possible if bulls stay above $0.95.

On the lower side, the liquidity pools (leveraged longs) were concentrated between $0.77 and $0.82, underscoring the possibility of price falling back to the Q3 price range if market sentiment worsens.

Taken together, SUI may continue to consolidate current Q3 and Binance hack-driven losses below $0.90. However, if U.S. midterm results and geopolitical tensions drive another risk-off move, there is a chance SUI could slip back to the Q3 price range and extend Q4 losses.

#5212. Binance Hacker Continues Aggressive Bitcoin Buying Spree

The hacker behind the monumental Binance attack is still buying up more Bitcoin (BTC).

As per the latest blockchain analytics data from Lookonchain dated Nov. 4, the hacker continues to exchange SOL for Bitcoin, moving the stolen assets across several crypto wallets.

The blockchain tracker added that the hacker is using the following multi-step process to exchange the stolen crypto for Bitcoin.

The hacker’s complicated scheme involves splitting his SOL assets among different wallets before selling the SOL for USDC, transferring the USDC from Solana to Ethereum, buying ETH using the USDC and finally exchanging the newly bought ETH for BTC using THORChain, a decentralized cross-chain liquidity protocol.

This long and complicated cross-chain transaction process allows the hacker to easily transfer his funds between different blockchain networks. It also makes it harder to trace the stolen assets.

This in not the first time that the hacker has been buying Bitcoin with the stolen funds. In fact, he had earlier been seen making transactions to buy Bitcoin using tens of millions of dollars’ worth of stolen SOL.

As per Lookonchain’s data dated Nov. 1, the hacker behind the Binance hack was trying to convert approximately 970,522 SOL, worth roughly $95.9 million at the time of writing, into Bitcoin.

The hacker was even seen making a transaction involving 486,970 SOL (approximately $46.4M) in just one hour.

However, during these transactions, the hacker used a different service called Relay Protocol, which also allows cross-chain crypto transfers.

From the recent blockchain data, it looks like the hacker is still focused on Bitcoin. He’s also using several decentralized services to exchange the stolen crypto.

The latest official update from Binance shows that the massive security breach has affected crypto assets worth around $6.34 billion.

As per the update, the exchange says that it has frozen/intercepted about $2.85 billion worth of stolen cryptocurrency as of Nov. 3.

According to US federal authorities, the attack was conducted by a North Korean state-sponsored hacker group called TraderTraitor.

The FBI is also asking various decentralized crypto services like THORChain to prevent funds linked to the attack from moving around.

Even with all these efforts, the latest on-chain data shows that the hacker is still moving stolen funds across different networks.

#5211. Evernorth Filing To Unlock 12 Million Shares For Resale

Home » Trendsetter News » Evernorth Filing To Unlock 12 Million Shares For Resale

Evernorth’s resale filing could potentially expand the tradable pool from 7 million to 19 million, analyst Dana Love says.

About 12 million additional Evernorth shares could become eligible for sale once a resale registration takes effect, according to crypto commentator Dana Love.

In a new video examining the XRP treasury company’s filings, Love argues that this potential supply matters more than the much larger registration headline & could reshape trading in a stock with a relatively small float.

XRPN fell from an opening price of $14.19 to $11.74 by early afternoon in the session discussed, a decline of roughly 17%. Love noted that the selloff followed the filing but stopped short of calling it the cause: “It’s a data point, not a verdict.”

The filing registers 73.7 million shares for resale across Evernorth’s capital structure. That does not mean all those shares are immediately available to sell.

Love’s calculation puts the existing tradable pool at roughly 7 million shares. Another approximately 12 million Class A shares could become eligible once the SEC declares the registration effective, potentially bringing that pool to about 19 million.

SBI Holdings owns 9.4 million Class A shares, or 36.1% of that class, according to the filing figures cited in the video. It is not among the holders subject to the six-month lockup ending around April 9; Ripple, the Arrington fund and the Larson trust are.

Eligibility is not intent. Love describes SBI’s investment as strategic and estimates that its main-round holdings were substantially underwater at the prices discussed.

The video also challenges share counts that exclude Evernorth’s other equity interests. Including Class A shares, Class C shares and exchangeable units, Love arrives at approximately 80.4 million shares against 473.3 million XRP – about 5.88 XRP per share.

At the video’s cited XRP price near $1.25, that represents roughly $7.35 in XRP per share. XRPN’s $11.74 price therefore still reflected a premium of about 60% to that backing.

Dana Love, PhD says the subscription agreements target registration effectiveness within 75 days of the October 9 closing, or December 23, with an extension possible if the SEC reviews the filing. That contractual timetable is not an SEC approval date.

Investors have two separate developments to watch: whether registered holders actually sell, and whether Evernorth uses its equity financing facility above XRP backing value to increase XRP per share.

More eligible stock could pressure the premium; issuing stock at a premium could improve the underlying XRP allocation.

The liquidation numbers don't prove that the outside events caused the sell-off. Traders cutting risks and high leverage could make the swings even bigger. If Bitcoin holds the $76,000 support zone, the market might calm down, but unfortunately, there's another big wave of liquidations sitting just around the corner in case of a breakdown.

#5207. 18 New XRP Millionaires Emerge in 72 Hours: Does It Signal a Price Bottom?

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

The current decline in XRP's market value has not led to loss-taking by large stakeholders — on the contrary, a phase of aggressive accumulation has unfolded in the market. According to data from the analytical platform Santiment, an absolute record for the current year has been set on the XRP Ledger network: 2,147 wallets now hold a balance of 1 million tokens or more.

The main inflow of capital came during the price correction period, when the asset's market price dropped to the $1.27 mark.

The main activity of large capital unfolded at the end of October. The dynamics with which investors are forming large positions points to a rigid strategic calculation: over the past ten days, 28 new addresses have attained millionaire status, and 18 of them were opened during the final three days of the reporting period.

Unlike speculative capital oriented toward quick profit, large holders traditionally use phases of price weakness for buying. The growth in the number of millionaires at such a moment confirms that large balances are being systematically increased rather than liquidated.

Such divergences between a falling chart and an inflow of institutional capital often precede the formation of a long-term market bottom.

Is this surge a signal that the price has found a bottom? Analysts identify three clear price thresholds that will give a precise answer to this question in the coming days:

• The $1.20 – $1.25 zone (Foundation): A critical support line and the main indicator of a potential bottom. If the price holds in this range and the number of millionaire wallets remains above 2,140, the long-term growth scenario will remain in force.

• The $1.30 milestone (Psychology): A local resistance level, a return above which is necessary to confirm a reversal and restore optimism in the mass market.

• The $1.40 level (Confirmation): Consolidation of the price above this mark amid a continuing inflow of large holders will prove that the bottom has been definitively passed and that capital is ready to drive the asset toward new highs.

Thus, the sustainability of XRP's current bullish setup depends directly on the ability of large capital to hold key support levels during the coming market movements.

#5205. Hoskinson’s Midnight Tease Raises a Hard Question for ADA Holders

Home » Trendsetter News » Hoskinson’s Midnight Tease Raises a Hard Question for ADA Holders

Charles Hoskinson’s undisclosed “magnum opus” could boost Midnight’s NIGHT token before benefiting ADA.

Charles Hoskinson’s next big reveal could benefit Midnight’s NIGHT token before it helps Cardano. That is the central argument from a crypto commentator examining the Cardano founder’s claim that he is developing the “magnum opus” of his career & whether its economic rewards will reach Cardano investors.

The project remains undisclosed in the video. Hoskinson reportedly says only Midnight can deliver it, leaving the host to assess several possibilities rather than a confirmed product announcement.

Each points toward the same investment question: where does activity create token demand? Midnight’s transaction resource, DUST, is generated by holding NIGHT, giving that token a closer connection to network usage than ADA has.

Fire Hustle cites Midnight network data showing daily transactions rising from fewer than 300 to more than 26,000 in roughly a week after permissionless application deployment opened. It reports more than 47,000 daily transactions by late October, although transaction counts alone do not establish sustained demand or economic value.

For ADA holders, the more direct potential benefit is a proposed rewards arrangement involving Cardano stake pools and their delegators. According to the host, opening Midnight participation to those operators is planned for early 2027; the rewards are not presented as already operating.

Fire Hustle puts ADA near $0.20 at recording, roughly 90% below its 2021 peak. The video also cites less than $100 million locked in Cardano applications and under $2,000 in network fees on one recent day – figures that underscore the gap between anticipation and current usage.

There is precedent for expectations outrunning delivery. ADA reached its 2021 high roughly 10 days before Cardano’s smart-contract upgrade, the host notes. That history is a warning about event-driven speculation, not proof that another sell-off will follow.

The distinction for ADA investors is between a compelling reveal and measurable value reaching their holdings. NIGHT may have the nearer-term exposure to Midnight adoption; ADA’s case depends more on whether staking rewards materialize and Cardano’s own application activity grows alongside it.

#5204. Solana Co-Founder Names Biggest Threat to Crypto

Solana co-founder Anatoly Yakovenko said phishing and social engineering attacks are the biggest threat to crypto and advised investors to worry more about social engineering attacks than about AI or quantum computers breaking crypto security.

"Phishing and social engineering is the biggest threat to crypto. Not AI breaking math or quantum computers," he wrote on X.

The Solana co-founder also suggested having a separate iOS device with Apple's Lockdown Mode turned on and using a multisignature crypto wallet.

This is one of the cheapest ways for investors to safeguard their digital assets, according to Yakovenko.

The Solana co-founder's statement highlights a fundamental weakness in crypto security: hackers don't need to break blockchain security to steal digital assets.

What they can do is fool crypto holders into giving them access to their assets.

Phishing involves using malicious emails, sites or messages to trick people into revealing sensitive information such as crypto wallet recovery phrases, account information, etc.

Social engineering is a broader term that describes attacks in which hackers manipulate victims into doing something that can compromise their security.

Yakovenko's point becomes crystal clear with the recent massive hack at Binance.

The world's largest crypto exchange, Binance, faced a security breach on Oct. 29 that affected around $6.34 billion in digital assets.

According to Binance's Nov. 3 investigation update, the hackers carried out a sophisticated social engineering attack, which included fake job interviews to compromise Binance employees.

A Binance software engineer was targeted by people pretending to be representatives of a fake company. The Binance employee went through some fake job interviews. The employee was convinced to download what seemed to be a legitimate technical assignment.

But the downloaded file contained malicious code that helped the hackers compromise the employee's computer.

Then, the hackers used the compromised devices, stolen credentials and an internal AI tool to access Binance's wallet infrastructure.

Eventually, this helped the hackers execute unauthorized crypto transfers from the exchange's hot wallets.

The Security Alliance (SEAL) released a report on Nov. 4 stating that its security team handled 99 incidents from Oct. 28 to Nov. 3.

In three of those incidents involving fraudulent customer support calls, victims lost roughly $1.57 million and 1.12 BTC from Oct. 28 to Nov. 3.

One of the scams involved fake Apple support representatives stealing a victim's Apple ID credentials. The victim had saved their crypto recovery phrase in cloud storage. Another victim shared their wallet recovery phrase during a fake Telegram support call.

The group also tracked 5,137 phishing domains during this reporting period.

TL;DR: The Hong Kong Monetary Authority and the Hong Kong University of Science and Technology announced a post-quantum cryptography assessment toolkit on November 2. Post-quantum migration is not the same thing as making every blockchain or bank system instantly quantum-secure.

Good security planning is often unglamorous. Discovering where keys are issued, which partners rely on them and when equipment will be replaced is difficult work, but it makes future transitions possible. The banking sector has a reason to start early because data captured today can remain sensitive long after the original connection has ended.

The Hong Kong Monetary Authority and the Hong Kong University of Science and Technology announced a post-quantum cryptography assessment toolkit on November 2. The initiative is aimed at helping banks understand where current cryptographic systems might need upgrades as quantum capabilities develop. It is a preparation programme, not a declaration that quantum computers have broken banking or cryptocurrency encryption. The dated original disclosure is the starting point for the figures and product scope discussed here.

Large organisations use cryptography in certificates, customer authentication, interbank links, software updates and the storage of sensitive data. Migrating those systems can take years because algorithms, devices, vendors and legacy applications must remain interoperable. A toolkit can help identify dependencies, prioritise sensitive data and test readiness for future standards before a forced emergency transition.

The HKMA has pointed to gaps in sector readiness and ambitions to improve preparedness by 2030. The most immediate benefit may be governance: teams can assign ownership of the inventory and agree which systems require the longest lead time. That is more useful than debating a speculative date on which a general-purpose quantum computer could break a particular cryptographic scheme.

A crypto incident produces several separate timelines: containment, investigation, recovery and a return to reliable service. Those phases can overlap without finishing together. A traced wallet is not automatically frozen; a frozen balance is not automatically returned; and a reopened withdrawal route is not a completed security audit. Keeping those differences visible helps readers judge progress more accurately.

For additional perspective, it is worth reading our existing reporting on Binance’s earlier staged withdrawal restart and Bitcoin payment infrastructure and node software context. Those background reports help separate established facts from a new, still-developing announcement.

Post-quantum migration is not the same thing as making every blockchain or bank system instantly quantum-secure. Different primitives face different levels of risk, and some proposed methods affect key size, verification performance or compatibility. The toolkit’s output is an assessment input, not a substitute for independent engineering and formal testing.

Operational controls matter most when a business is under stress. Security announcements should identify the system affected, the customers who can act, the evidence supporting any recovery number and what remains uncertain. Clear scope protects users from false reassurance and from unnecessary panic. It also provides a sensible basis for follow-up accountability.

Watch whether participating institutions publish migration road maps, begin procurement changes and test post-quantum authentication with core systems. For crypto readers, it is a reminder to separate near-term operational security from longer-term cryptographic research. No credible reading of the announcement implies that Bitcoin private keys are currently being routinely cracked by quantum machines.

#5202. Morgan Stanley and Chainlink advance tokenization – But will demand follow?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

The trend of moving towards tokenizing traditional markets is accelerating as large financial organizations begin to implement blockchain-based systems.

Morgan Stanley has begun its transition to implementing such technology, joining forces with DTCC by utilizing its new tokenization service.

In fact, DTCC launched its DTC Tokenization Service (DTS) into live production on the 28th of October. The service utilizes blockchain technology to convert securities held by DTCC clients into custody into tokens.

If successful, it could increase the liquidity of equities and facilitate better usage of collateral in lending transactions.

In addition, Morgan Stanley’s involvement represents a significant step forward from experimental projects into practical institutional adoption. The next challenge will be to integrate these tokenized assets with existing market infrastructure.

Commenting on the move, Amy Oldenburg, the bank’s Head of Digital Assets Strategy, said the hands-on integration experience will inform the firm’s digital asset development as markets evolve.

This highlights how practical implementation could shape wider institutional adoption.

That institutional push is also reaching blockchain data providers. This is as Chainlink [LINK] expands access to Asian stock markets.

Its APAC Equities Streams now cover over $40 trillion in equity markets across China, Japan, South Korea, Taiwan, and Hong Kong. The feeds provide live prices for companies including TSMC, Tencent, Samsung, SK Hynix, and BYD.

This gives blockchain platforms access to traditional stock market data. The feeds are already available on Lighter, Jupiter Exchange, and Predict, supporting trading and prediction markets linked to traditional stocks.

Together, these developments show how banks and crypto firms are bringing financial assets onto blockchain networks, although wider adoption will depend on demand.

As institutional participation and on-chain access expand, actual market activity will determine whether tokenization gains lasting traction. To achieve this, rising trading volume must lead to consistent settlement and increasing transaction counts.

If not for sustained settlement activity, then creating more options in terms of asset availability does not necessarily result in increased demand.

Therefore, distinguishing between institutional adoption and market adoption is important when assessing adoption metrics. Morgan Stanley and DTCC are working to make traditional assets accessible to blockchain-type networks.

However, simply making an asset accessible via blockchain does not automatically create a sustainable demand for that product. Similarly, Chainlink is providing data necessary for on-chain trades.

However, infrastructure is only one component needed to create active markets. For instance, repeated trading activity by investors will support the creation of liquid markets. Additionally, higher levels of liquidity will reduce friction associated with completing trades.

Ultimately, tokenization’s success will depend less on how many assets reach blockchain networks and more on how often people use them.

#5201. XRP’s Rebound Faces a Tougher Test Than Breaking $1.30

Home » Ripple (XRP) » XRP’s Rebound Faces a Tougher Test Than Breaking $1.30

The XRP Ledger inked nearly 8K new wallets in a day, but big-time bounce back potential depends on more than that.

XRP has edged back toward $1.30 after a bruising October, but the recovery is running into a contradiction: network activity is improving while demand from U.S. trading venues appears to be fading.

The token traded near $1.25 to $1.28 in the latest reports, recovering modestly as broader crypto markets remained cautious. One market analysis pointed to a bullish Bollinger Band setup and a potential larger upside move, though that view depends on XRP holding its recent gains in an unsettled market.

The XRP Ledger added 7,934 wallets on November 1, according to Santiment data cited in market coverage. That was among the network’s strongest daily readings of the year and notable because it occurred on a typically quieter weekend.

Some observers linked the increase in self-custodial wallets to the recent Binance security breach, which shook confidence across the market. Yet more addresses have not translated into a clear change in trading psychology: bearish commentary on XRP still outweighs bullish sentiment, and the token remains down sharply over the past month.

That split matters. New wallets can signal growing usage or a shift toward self-custody, but they do not by themselves establish sustained buying pressure. For a more convincing recovery, XRP would likely need to reclaim $1.30 while daily network growth remains elevated.

Signals from the U.S. market are less encouraging. XRP’s Coinbase Premium Index fell to roughly negative 0.16%, an eight-month low, meaning the asset traded at a discount on Coinbase relative to Binance. Such a discount can point to weaker demand among U.S. retail and institutional participants, although it is not a definitive forecast of further declines.

Spot XRP exchange-traded funds also slowed in October, drawing $18.83 million in net inflows, compared with $121.40 million in September. The funds remained relatively stable around the late-October market disruption, but the slower pace suggests fresh capital has become more selective.

#5200. Can You Put Crypto Mining in Space? Elon Musk's Data Center Idea Can Be Used in Crypto

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Elon Musk did what he does best on X: fresh speculations via post on November 4, saying: “Data centers in space look pretty appealing right now.”

Musk was talking about data centers, but the idea can be used in crypto too: could Bitcoin mining rigs work in orbit, powered only by the Sun? Technically, they should, why not? Economically, however, it's getting a bit more complicated.

Space has one big advantage: plenty of solar energy. Unlike mining farms on Earth, orbital mining stations could use large solar ray to make their own electricity without any power grid. Satellites today could get constant powering from sunlight, leading to a lower battery consumption.

Data centers in space looking pretty appealing right now

In theory, Bitcoin ASIC miners could then run around the clock with very low or non-existent ongoing electricity costs. The problem is that solar panels, batteries, power electronics and would all have to be launched into orbit–that's the part where problems arise.

Space might look like the perfect place to cool mining equipment, since the cosmic background is extremely cold. Unfortunately, it's not so simple and the vacuum in space actually makes cooling harder than on earth.

Here, mining rigs get rid of heat with fans, air conditioning or liquid cooling, in orbit, there's no air at all that will be able to push the air away. So the only option out there seems to be the liquid cooling, large radiator panels which should be able to release heat as infrared radiation.

According to Boston Consulting Group (BCG), a 100-kilowatt orbital data center could need around a whopping 400 square meters of radiators. But apart from technical challenges, we should always keep in mind the profitability. Getting equipment to space in the first place is what makes the whole idea questionable.

According to BCG, launching to orbit currently costs around $1,500 per kilogram. That means, a one-ton mining setup would cost around $1.5 million just to launch, excluding hardware, cooling, solar arrays and maintenance that will cost just as much if not more.

You also can't ignore space radiation, equipment failures and limited chances for repairs that add even more costs at the top of it. The only thing that can somehow make math look better is more efficient ASIC chips and ways of delivering goods to space at the fraction of today's costs.

Thankfully, Bitcoin miners need little to no bandwidth, so the connection issue is somewhat solved on its own.

Physically, mining Bitcoin in space is possible: is it justifiable financially though? Not really. Reusable rockets, lightweight cooling and other things might make it more financially viable, but as of today, we should be sticking with good old mining rigs in huge hangars on Earth.

#5199. Coinbase Explains Multi-User Architecture as Crypto Accounts Become More Complex

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Coinbase published an engineering account of its move toward multi-user infrastructure on October 29, describing how one account or organisation can involve several people with different privileges. A blog describing architecture cannot prove that every application and endpoint is free of bugs.

Account controls rarely make headlines until something fails. But the ability to limit who can initiate and approve a transaction is one of the main differences between a personal wallet and an institution-ready service. Coinbase’s design discussion is a reminder that software architecture can shape real financial risk.

Coinbase published an engineering account of its move toward multi-user infrastructure on October 29, describing how one account or organisation can involve several people with different privileges. The company discussed a distinction between the person acting and the entity on whose behalf an action occurs. This is a technical design explanation, not a claim that all customer products immediately acquired the same administrative features. The dated original disclosure is the starting point for the figures and product scope discussed here.

This matters because crypto businesses increasingly resemble financial organisations with staff, service providers and internal controls. A trading desk may want one employee to prepare an operation and another to approve it. Clear identity and authorisation boundaries are essential to making those workflows auditable. They are also an everyday security feature, not merely an enterprise convenience.

A crypto incident produces several separate timelines: containment, investigation, recovery and a return to reliable service. Those phases can overlap without finishing together. A traced wallet is not automatically frozen; a frozen balance is not automatically returned; and a reopened withdrawal route is not a completed security audit. Keeping those differences visible helps readers judge progress more accurately.

This development also connects to two subjects our newsroom has been following: Binance’s earlier staged withdrawal restart and Bitcoin payment infrastructure and node software context. They explain related market structure and should not be mistaken for evidence that every new proposal is already live.

A blog describing architecture cannot prove that every application and endpoint is free of bugs. Permission checks can be correct in one service and inconsistent elsewhere, especially during migration. Nor does a multi-user model replace strong authentication, careful device management or transaction-specific controls on sensitive funds movement.

Operational controls matter most when a business is under stress. Security announcements should identify the system affected, the customers who can act, the evidence supporting any recovery number and what remains uncertain. Clear scope protects users from false reassurance and from unnecessary panic. It also provides a sensible basis for follow-up accountability.

The next valuable evidence will be product-level rollout details, documented access roles and whether enterprise customers can adopt least-privilege policies without workarounds. The broader lesson is that the security of a digital-asset account often depends as much on internal identity systems as on the cryptography protecting a blockchain transaction.

#5198. SEC gives Canton crypto regulatory relief – Why CC faces a $0.10 test

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

On the 3rd of November, the SEC Division of Corporation Finance reported that it views Canton [CC] as a digital commodity. Through a no-action letter, the division said that Canton’s value derives from the Canton Network’s operations and market dynamics.

Simply put, the SEC clarified that Canton doesn’t get value from profits generated by others’ efforts. Furthermore, the division added that the coin lacks a central party.

For that reason, the division noted that,

“ The division will not recommend enforcement action to the commission”

The decision reflects the division’s position based on facts presented by Canton. However, it does mean the SEC has made conclusions on whether the coin is a security.

The SEC decision was welcomed by market players. Miles Jennings, for example, added that the SEC’s token taxonomy from March and follow-up FAQs created a clear pathway out of securities laws.

SEC’s decision provides legal clarity for institutions rushing to tokenize on the Canton Network. In fact, the network reported that institutions have been extremely active in recent months.

Firstly, DTCC launched its tokenization service with 20 participants, including JPMorgan and Fidelity for tokenized stocks.

Secondly, Uber piloted treasury cash moves using HSBC tokenized deposits and Franklin Templeton funds. Finally, Taiwan’s TDCC joined the Canton Foundation.

These institutions join an expanding base for tokenized assets. Canton Network reported that it leads the rankings for tokenized assets.

According to RWA.xyz, RWA value on Canton is more than seven times that of the next four chains combined, with a value of around $348.9 billion. Moreover, the rising value points to growing adoption and network acceptance by major institutions.

On the charts, the market responded strongly positively to the SEC’s decision. Notably, Canton bounced back from a $0.093 slip to a local high of $0.0986.

On the daily charts, the altcoin is up 5% while the trading volume climbed 121% to $31 million. Despite these gains, the market is still experiencing strong selling pressure.

On the spot, for instance, the Sell Volume exceeded 9 million over the past 24 hours compared to 8 million in volume, implyinh that more sell orders were executed.

The same was observed on the perps side, with Sell Volume rising to 8.1 million on Binance.

The high volume indicated that investors have yet to flip with the trend and are mostly fearful of another drop, thus cashing out recent gains.

Under such circumstances, CC is unlikely to sustain the uptrend. If seller dominance persists, CC could pull back to $0.092.

However, if the trend reversal holds with investors turning optimistic over the legal clarity, CC will flip $0.1 and eye $0.11 in the short term.

#5197. Dogecoin Contributor Shares Key Safety Reminder Amid Binance Withdrawal Resumption

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Dogecoin community member Mishaboar has shared a fresh safety reminder to DOGE holders as cryptocurrency withdrawals resume on Binance following the exchange's recent security incident.

Binance began restoring withdrawals in phases on November 1, 2026, with that of Dogecoin already restored. Binance has also published a cryptographic Proof of Reserves for selected assets, including Dogecoin, based on its October 31 snapshot.

A few updates on the Binance situation since my last post. - The Hack Binance has finally published its initial technical findings, and, as we had expected, the attack was considerably more complicated than the original explanation involving a compromised devops laptop. According to Binance, the attackers targeted multiple engineers through malicious software, including an earlier attempt involving a tampered devtool installer and a fake job interview that delivered malware. They then exploited an internal AI tool and stolen deployment credentials to gain access to Binance's wallet-signing infrastructure. On Ethereum, malicious EIP-7702 authorizations allowed the attackers to continue moving funds even after Binance had suspended normal withdrawals. It's difficult to understand why the signing controls allowed these authorizations in the first place. I am curious to know how it is possible the authorizations were not detected more quickly, and why revoking them took so long on wallets holding such gigantic amounts. Binance says it took almost four hours to fully contain the attack. It will be good to see independent reviews from competent security analysts. - Withdrawals/Deposits (after withdrawals reopened) According to figures shared by Binance co-CEO Richard Teng and reported by CoinDesk, approximately $10.97 billion was withdrawn during the first 46 hours after withdrawals reopened, against $5.69 billion in deposits. That leaves approximately $5.28 billion in net outflows, as of early November 3. Withdrawal activity was particularly intense on Sunday, but appears to have slowed down since. Binance claims that hourly deposits had started exceeding withdrawals by the end of that period. - The Money Binance reported approximately $2.85 billion frozen or intercepted in its November 3 update. Once again, remember that frozen funds are NOT necessarily recovered funds. Binance also says it has replaced the vast majority of affected assets using its own resources, including SAFU and insurance funds (see my previous update for considerations about this). The published replenishment percentages have been somewhat inconsistent, however. And once again, replacing stolen assets is NOT the same as recovering them from the attackers. Using SAFU and the Futures Insurance Fund to cover these losses also means that the risk profile of holding funds on the exchange may have shifted considerably. As exchanges operate largely within black boxes, it is difficult to assess this at this point. - Withdrawals and Proof of Reserves Binance has published a cryptographic Proof of Reserves for ten selected assets (including Dogecoin), based on its October 31 snapshot. That is considerably fewer than the 55 assets covered in its previous snapshot, with many smaller assets still missing. Once again, this is useful, but as I have explained multiple times, Proof of Reserves are never a comprehensive audit of an exchange's financial condition and stability. More cryptocurrency withdrawals are being restored in phases, although availability still depends on the specific asset and network. Native Dogecoin withdrawals were included in the first reopening phase. The usual reminder: please make sure you select the actual Dogecoin network when withdrawing to your own wallet, rather than stuff like Binance-pegged DOGE on BSC. My recommendation stays the same: withdraw when and if you can, without rushing, and after verifying that you have good backups of your wallets and that the wallet you are using is safe.

As native Dogecoin withdrawals have now been restored, Mishaboar shared his "usual reminder" to the Dogecoin community, telling them to ensure they select the actual Dogecoin network when withdrawing to their wallets, "rather than stuff like Binance-pegged DOGE on BSC," he said.

A Binance-Pegged Dogecoin, a BEP-20 token, runs on the BNB Smart Chain (BSC), and the distinction between it and the actual DOGE coin on the Dogecoin network remains essential to prevent confusion.

Mishaboar also advised users not to rush the withdrawal process, noting that wallet security and proper backup procedures should come first: "My recommendation remains the same: withdraw when and if you can, without rushing, and after verifying that you have good backups of your wallets and that the wallet you are using is secure."

Major crypto exchange Binance recently published initial findings and a response update on the October 29 security incident.

According to Binance, the attackers targeted multiple engineers through malicious software, including an earlier attempt involving a tampered devtool installer and a fake job interview that delivered malware. They then exploited an internal AI tool and stolen deployment credentials to gain access to Binance's wallet-signing infrastructure.

The incident involves assets of some $6.34 billion. About $2.85 billion worth of assets have been intercepted or frozen as of the update.

As of Oct. 31, 2026, Binance said it has restored 98% of the affected token assets and is working on purchasing outstanding, more illiquid assets.

Binance stated that the entry points identified as having been used in the attack have been closed, and it is engaging independent third-party cybersecurity experts to conduct a comprehensive review of the incident.
