#5164. Cardano Eyes 2,000% Replay From Current Base

Home » Crypto Market News » Cardano Eyes 2,000% Replay From Current Base

As 131 million ADA tokens sit idle in retired pools, the weekly chart structure points to $1.37 as next-cycle destination.

Cardano is trading near $0.19 on a weekly chart that one analyst compares directly to the base that preceded a roughly 2,000% rally in the last cycle. At the same time, on-chain data shows 131.63 million ADA remains tied to retired stake pools, leaving tens of thousands of holders without rewards until they redelegate.

360Trader highlighted the weekly structure and noted that ADA remains well below the level where a true bull-market stage would begin. That stage, in the analyst’s view, only starts once price clears $1.37.

The more immediate requirement is a reclaim of $0.25. Until then the chart is still forming the same kind of low base that previously launched a 2,000% advance once the broader altcoin market turned. The comparison is structural rather than a near-term price target.

Chart Request winner: $ADA 📊Weekly chart, and it's still well under its bull-market stage. That stage really doesn't start until $1.37 breaks.First it needs to get back above $0.25 quickly before I'd be interested.Last cycle ADA went 2000% off a base just like this one.… pic.twitter.com/4BPAusTmTY

From current levels a move to the prior cycle high would still require a multi-hundred-percent gain, and the larger $1.37 threshold sits even further away.

ADA has not yet reclaimed $0.25, the level 360Trader said would be needed before the setup becomes interesting again. The token has been consolidating near the lower end of its multi-year range while the wider crypto market digests recent pressure.

The analyst’s point is that the base itself is not the signal. Confirmation only arrives when price begins to push out of the accumulation zone and the broader altcoin cycle starts to expand.

Until those conditions appear, the historical 2,000% precedent remains a reminder of what has happened from comparable structures rather than a forecast for the next few weeks.

In related Cardano news, Cexplorer data flagged a practical on-chain issue. Roughly 59,025 delegators still have funds assigned to 2,720 retired stake pools, totaling 131.63 million ADA.

Those pools no longer produce blocks or distribute rewards. Holders retain full control of their tokens and can move the delegation to an active pool at any time. The funds are not locked or lost, but every epoch spent on a retired pool is simply a missed reward.

Cardano Has a 131M ADA Staking ProblemThousands of Cardano (@Cardano) holders may need to check where their $ADA is delegated.Cexplorer reports that 59,025 delegators still have funds assigned to retired stake pools. The total comes to 131.63 million ADA.The explorer… pic.twitter.com/cdTn46aFLr

Community responses noted that checking a pool’s status on an explorer and redelegating takes only a minute and restores eligibility for the next epoch’s rewards.

The technical observation and the staking data sit side by side. One points to a historical base that once produced a 2,000% move once conditions aligned.

The other shows that a measurable portion of the staked supply is currently idle because pool operators retired and holders have not yet updated their delegations.

ADA remains near $0.19, below the $0.25 reclaim level and far from the $1.37 threshold that would mark a shift into a broader bull-market phase.

Whether the base develops the same way as last cycle depends on price reclaiming those levels and on the wider altcoin market providing the liquidity. In the meantime, any Cardano holder still delegated to a retired pool can restore rewards by simply choosing an active one.

#5163. Bitcoin rockets above $80,000 for first time since Binance hack despite Democrats sweep, but a historical crash looms

Bitcoin has risen after every US midterm since 2010, although previous elections triggered brutal selloffs before the cryptocurrency recovered.

Bitcoin surged more than 5% from its election-night low to reclaim $80,000 for the first time since Binance's record hack.

The cryptocurrency extended its recovery from approximately $75,600 on Wednesday, shaking off concerns that Democrats' takeover of Congress would undermine President Donald Trump's crypto-friendly agenda.

According to CoinGlass data, this rally liquidated $453.32 million in the past hour, with most of the losses coming from short traders. These losses reach $913.34 million over the past 24 hours.

CoinGlass heatmap screenshot supplied Nov. 4, 2026. Only tiles with a readable dollar value are included; smaller coins are omitted. 'Other' is the heatmap's labeled group, not a calculated remainder. BTC liquidations were 96.3% short and ETH 57.6% short. Values are rounded liquidated position amounts, not traders' net losses. Snapshot time was not shown.

Source: CoinGlass liquidation heatmap (1h, user screenshot, Nov. 4, 2026)

The rally comes less than a week after Binance suffered a $6.34 billion security breach that sent shockwaves through the digital asset market, triggering withdrawals and intensifying an already difficult period for Bitcoin investors.

James Butterfill, head of research at CoinShares, told CryptoSlate that the Democratic sweep had largely been priced into Bitcoin before the election, creating room for a relief rally as political uncertainty dissipated.

His assessment reflects a growing distinction between the potential consequences of divided government for the wider crypto industry and Bitcoin itself.

Butterfill expects Democratic control to complicate efforts to advance the CLARITY Act, while potential investigations into Trump-linked cryptocurrency businesses could divert political attention from regulatory reform.

However, he argued that exchanges, token issuers and other companies requiring new regulatory permissions would be more vulnerable to those developments than Bitcoin.

He said that the cryptocurrency's direction remains more closely tied to real yields, dollar liquidity and exchange-traded product flows.

The broader financial markets have already begun reflecting that shift in attention.

According to institutional trading platform Talos, Bitcoin's DVOL, a gauge of expected volatility, declined 6%, while Wall Street's VIX fell 10% as reduced uncertainty encouraged systematic buying across risk assets.

The retreat in volatility coincided with a weaker dollar and declining Treasury yields, providing a more favorable backdrop for Bitcoin's recovery.

On-chain data suggests the rally is arriving as the selling pressure that dominated Bitcoin's recent decline begins to weaken.

CryptoQuant analyst Darkfost said the firm's seller exhaustion model has fallen to levels historically associated with bear-market lows.

The indicator compares the proportion of Bitcoin supply held at a profit with average monthly volatility to assess whether existing holders still have enough incentive to keep selling.

Its latest reading follows a correction of more than 10% from Bitcoin's previous high, during which short-term holders appear to have realized profits and reduced their exposure.

Previous episodes of similarly depressed selling activity have coincided with the formation of market bottoms, although the indicator offers no assurance that prices will immediately recover.

Darkfost identified a critical weakness in the current market: buyers have yet to demonstrate the same conviction as the sellers who recently exited. He said:

Start with the stories that matter. Get the context behind the headlines and a clear view of what to watch next.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

“Even if sellers have calmed down, demand is still needed, and Bitcoin is still short of it.”

That concern is especially relevant after three consecutive trading sessions of withdrawals from US spot Bitcoin ETFs, which have deprived the market of an important source of institutional demand.

While the retreat in selling pressure has allowed Bitcoin to recover above $80,000, sustaining that advance will require fresh capital to absorb any renewed profit-taking.

Bitcoin's latest recovery also coincides with a historical pattern that has delivered substantial gains following every US midterm election since 2010.

CryptoQuant data shows the flagship crypto asset has risen over the subsequent 12 months in all four election cycles, generating a median return of 63%.

Those gains occurred under different political arrangements, including the 2018 midterms, when Democrats captured the House while Republicans retained the Senate.

Bitcoin gained 45.8% over the following year, while the comparable returns after the 2014 and 2022 elections reached 36.8% and 80.8%, respectively.

However, those annual figures conceal significant losses suffered by investors who bought immediately after the votes.

Bitcoin plunged 49.7% within approximately six weeks of the 2018 election before recovering. The cryptocurrency suffered a 39.8% drawdown following the 2014 vote and an 18.5% decline after the 2022 midterms, when the collapse of FTX destabilized the market.

Low percentage-change markers transcribed from the supplied CryptoQuant 365-day post-midterm chart. These are post-election lows, not single-day losses or dollar losses. Figures are shown to one decimal point as labeled; the dashboard did not expose raw data or precise calculation conventions. Other closing-price series differ, notably for 2014 and 2022. The 2026 cycle is excluded.

Source: CryptoQuant community dashboard (user-supplied screenshot, Nov. 4, 2026)

The apparent election effect also coincides with another recurring feature of Bitcoin's history.

US midterms occur every four years, roughly matching Bitcoin's halving schedule. CryptoQuant found that each election since 2010 occurred about 17 to 25 months before the next halving.

The 2014, 2018 and 2022 votes followed major bear markets, meaning the subsequent rallies unfolded as Bitcoin emerged from steep declines.

That relationship faces a different test in 2026.

Bitcoin entered Tuesday's election with a 20.4% gain over the preceding three months, compared with losses of 44% in 2014, 8.3% in 2018 and 16.5% in 2022.

That leaves this year's market in a materially different position from the previous three cycles, potentially weakening the relevance of the historical post-election returns.

Talos' seasonal figures provide another complication. Bitcoin lost 7.4% in October, and November subsequently recorded declines in three of the five previous years when October ended lower, including 2018 and 2025.

That is why Kelly Ye, Talos' head of research, cautioned against extrapolating the initial relief rally into a sustained recovery. She said:

“First reactions aren't always the right ones.”

Oluwapelumi values Bitcoin's potential. He imparts insights on a range of topics like DeFi, hacks, mining and culture, underlining transformative power.

#5162. BMNR stock jumps 7% as Bitmine closes in on owning 5% of all ETH

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Bitmine Immersion Technologies [BMNR] just bought more Ethereum [ETH]. At this point, it’s a habit. However, the pile isn’t negligible anymore, making this worthy enough to take a look.

Bitmine picked up 8,573 ETH last week, at $2,261 per token. That’s worth nearly $20 million! At press time, the total stash was worth 6,060,506 ETH.

In fact, that’s 4.9% of Ethereum’s 122.2 million supply. The company has been chasing 5% for about 15 months and now, they’re about 50,000 ETH short.

According to Chairman Tom Lee, Bitmine has bought ETH every single week, since starting its treasury strategy in June 2025.

Bitmine’s track record of consistent buying of crypto is unmatched by any public company in the world.

Most of that ETH is working too. Around 5.23 million tokens, or 86% of the stack, are staked. That’s worth about $11.8 billion.

At full scale, it is expected that they’d bring about $415 million a year in staking rewards. Add cash, Bitcoin [BTC], and a few side bets, then Bitmine’s total holdings come up to $14.5 billion.

Lee also commented on Binance’s recent $6.3 billion hack, which the FBI has blamed on North Korea. Comparing it to the Bybit hack of 2025, he stated,

We believe this is a far more muted market reaction despite the size of the theft being approximately quadruple the size…

The stock seems to like what it’s hearing as well.

BMNR stock was at $21.90 on 4th November, having started the day at $20.50. A jump of nearly 7% in a few hours, with a high just above $22.20.

It’s not been a smooth road though. On the 2nd, it slid to about $19.45 before bouncing. It then spent most of 3rd November between $20.30 to $20.80.

The near-term has been looking a bit hotter too. The RSI on the 5-minute view was above 80 during the run, and has since slowed to 68.5. The MACD was above the Signal Line at press time, but the green bars were shrinking.

Simply put, buyers might still be in charge, but it didn’t seem very obvious.

#5161. Bitbase Launches ORIGIN, a Points Season That Rewards Consistency

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

PANAMA CITY, November 4, 2026 — Bitbase today launched ORIGIN, the first season of its Bitbase Points program, which is designed to reward sustained participation over time. Over the 26-week season, which ends on May 4, 2027, Bitbase will distribute 1,000,000 points each week, allocated daily according to each participant’s contribution.

Each participant’s contribution is drawn from four sources: trading, assets held on Bitbase, consistent activity and referrals. Consistent activity is counted as a separate source, so continued participation from day to day adds to a user’s contribution. All eligible users, whether new to Bitbase or already active on the platform, are enrolled automatically.

For each new user referred directly, the referrer receives an additional 25% of that user’s contribution. The new user’s own points are not affected.

Participants will also receive a Trading Identity card, a shareable summary of their trading style. The first cards will be issued on November 9, with new cards following each Monday for the remainder of the season.

Existing users begin with points already credited

Before the season began, Bitbase credited points to eligible existing users based on their earlier activity on the platform. As a result, these users enter ORIGIN with their prior activity already reflected in their points and ranking.

On November 2, the exchange introduced Bitbase 2.0, updating its brand, app and website under the statement “Market Flows. We Focus.” ORIGIN is the first program to put that statement into practice. While markets move continuously, Bitbase has chosen to assess participation across months rather than individual moments.

“We designed ORIGIN for users who participate consistently, not only during the busiest periods in the market,” said Nicholas Lo, Chief Business Officer at Bitbase. “Over a six-month season, that consistency carries weight. Our responsibility is to make sure every contribution is credited, from the first day of the season to the last.”

Points and rankings are available in the Bitbase app and at bitbase.com/points.

Bitbase is a digital asset exchange serving individual and institutional users worldwide, with products spanning Spot, Futures, TradFi and Earn. Bitbase aims to give users a stable and straightforward trading experience, in line with its brand statement, “Market Flows. We Focus.” Learn more at bitbase.com.

#5160. Riot Liquidates 79% of Its Bitcoin Treasury for $28 Billion AI Infrastructure Deal

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

American Riot Platforms Inc. (RIOT) sold off 79% of its Bitcoin in the third quarter of 2026, redirecting capital toward building infrastructure for artificial intelligence. According to a fresh financial report, the company reduced its cryptocurrency reserves from 11,380 to 2,418 BTC.

In total, over the first nine months of the year, Riot sold 20,264 BTC on the market, receiving about $1.5 billion for them. This money went toward financing a major technological shift: the company's free energy capacity is being repurposed for high-performance computing needs.

Riot shares reacted to the session open with a sharp jump to $16.20, after which they stabilized in the $15.45–$15.50 range, holding positions on elevated volumes.

The main incentive for selling the cryptocurrency was a 15-year agreement to lease ready-made sites and capacities (powered land lease) totaling 910 MW at the Corsicana campus in Texas. The strategic partner was the Frontier AI Lab (Anthropic) laboratory.

The initial cost of the contract is estimated at $28 billion, and the average annual rent at the start will be about $1.8 billion. Taking into account other projects, Riot's total long-term revenue in the data center segment has reached $38 billion, and the total controlled capacity is 1,151 MW.

Riot CEO Jason Les noted that the agreement is structured as a triple-net lease. Under its terms, the client itself purchases and maintains the computing equipment. For Riot, this reduces construction costs by roughly half compared to standard Tier 3 data centers. As a result, the company will be able to do without issuing new shares and diluting investors' stakes.

The shift in focus is occurring against the backdrop of falling margins in classic mining. Riot's quarterly revenue from cryptocurrency mining fell by 28% — to $116 million, although the company actually mined 1,637 BTC.

Nevertheless, total quarterly revenue held at $181.4 million (+1% year-over-year), outpacing market forecasts. Net profit amounted to $4.03 million.

To optimize its balance sheet, the company fully closed its $200 million credit line with Coinbase, returning 5,821 BTC from collateral. Riot ended the quarter with free liquidity of $950.2 million (including cash and the remainder of the cryptocurrency). The balance sheet retains 2,418 BTC, which may also go under the hammer in the fourth quarter to finance the Texas construction.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

The Spot Bitcoin [BTC] ETFs witnessed a sustained wave of outflows between the 30th of October and the 3rd of November, recording approximately $1.16 billion in cumulative net outflows in just three trading sessions.

According to Farside Investors data, on the 30th of October, the ETFs recorded their largest single-day outflow of the period, worth $643.2 million.

In fact, the negative momentum continued on the 2nd of November, when BTC ETFs recorded another $398.6 million in net outflows. On the 3rd of November, however, the outflow momentum eased considerably, with total withdrawals declining to $115.6 million.

In these three days, BlackRock’s IBIT alone accounted for $734 million in outflows, making it the biggest contributor to the negative trend.

On the other hand, spot Ethereum [ETH] ETFs continued to experience persistent capital outflows for seven consecutive days from the 26th of October to the 3rd of November 2026. Overall, Ethereum spot ETFs recorded approximately $656.4 million in cumulative net outflows.

Things were looking pretty good into the end of September but a chunk of that reversed course in October.

Meanwhile, on the altcoin front, XRP ETFs witnessed a $529.51K net outflow on the 2nd of November.

In contrast, Solana [SOL] spot ETFs recorded zero flows on both days.

All this happened amidst a tough macro backdrop, where the U.S. Federal Reserve’s October decision to maintain interest rates at 3.75%–4.00% ignited concerns about persistent inflation.

Meanwhile, the 3rd November U.S. midterm elections added to market caution, while a modest recovery in the U.S. dollar further pressured dollar-denominated assets.

Topping all of this was the Binance security breach that triggered fresh anxiety across the cryptocurrency market, potentially accelerating institutional withdrawals.

#5158. Kamino links most of CEO’s KMNO vesting to valuation while holders have no foundation revenue claim

The CEO’s vesting incentive and a $20 million growth commitment leave capital allocation between foundation earnings and any future holder benefit.

Michael Weisz, CEO of Kamino, has tied most of his KMNO allocation to sustained token valuations, according to the Solana lending protocol’s November 3 disclosure. The same announcement tells holders that owning the token gives them no claim on foundation revenue, distributions or equity.

Kamino’s November 3 release describes both the CEO’s incentive arrangement and a $20 million foundation commitment to support its long-term growth plans over the next 24 months. It leaves capital allocation as the step between money accruing to the foundation and any future financial benefit for holders.

Most of Weisz’s KMNO allocation vests only when progressively higher valuation milestones are reached and sustained, the release says. A temporary increase in price does not satisfy the requirement.

That condition concerns most of his allocation. The release does not say that every founder, contributor or investor allocation follows the same rules, and it supplies neither the numerical milestones nor the period for which each must be sustained.

For holders, the incentive aligns part of the CEO’s compensation with sustained token valuations.

Kamino says the foundation has no shareholders, members or beneficiaries, operates under an independent board, and receives all onchain and offchain revenue and intellectual property. Its description places the protocol’s earnings and the foundation’s broader resources within one organization.

The release then states the limit on what that structure means for holders: “KMNO is not a claim on Foundation revenue, distributions or equity.”

DefiLlama’s Kamino data, captured at 20:10 UTC on November 3, makes the economic distinction concrete. Over the preceding 30 days, it recorded $5 million in fees, $607,996 in protocol revenue and $0 in tracked holder revenue.

Each number answers a different question. Fees measure charges generated by tracked activity. Protocol revenue measures the portion retained by the protocol. Holder revenue tracks value reaching holders, rather than assuming everything the protocol earns reaches tokenholders.

For Kamino’s lending products, DefiLlama includes interest, liquidation charges and origination charges in fees. Revenue includes interest spreads, part of liquidation charges and all origination charges; for liquidity products, it includes the protocol’s share of liquidity fees.

Tracked protocol earnings, foundation resources and tokenholder rights describe different economic relationships.

KMNO-linked executive incentives and foundation revenue do not give holders a revenue, distribution or equity claim.

Protocol data is not a foundation financial statement. Zero tracked holder revenue does not measure every holder benefit. Foundation governance, revenue accrual and plans are issuer disclosures; no price outcome or future distribution is assumed.

Reported trailing-30-day figures. Bar lengths compare $607,996 revenue with $4.67m fees (revenue/fees = 0.130192; desktop 620 and 80.72 units, mobile 310 and 40.36). Organization connectors describe issuer-reported relationships, not a quantified money transfer.

Protocol data is not a foundation financial statement. Zero tracked holder revenue does not measure every holder benefit. Foundation governance, revenue accrual and plans are issuer disclosures; no price outcome or future distribution is assumed.

The $5 million fee total therefore cannot be read as foundation income available for distribution. Even the narrower $607,996 protocol-revenue figure is not a complete statement of foundation earnings, particularly given Kamino’s description of offchain revenue accruing there too.

KMNO’s market snapshot gives scale to the holder-value question: at 20:12 UTC on November 3, CryptoSlate displayed a price of about $0.034, a $200 million market capitalization and $20 million in 24-hour trading volume.

Kamino says the foundation holds reserves beyond its $20 million growth commitment. That commitment describes spending on expansion over two years, rather than a distribution to tokenholders or revenue already earned.

For someone evaluating KMNO, protocol revenue shows what tracked operations retain. Assessing a future holder benefit requires a further explanation of how foundation resources would be allocated, including what is spent on growth, retained in reserves or used in a potential token-support program.

The release cautions that future plans, including potential programs supporting KMNO, may change. Holders can assess the growth commitment and executive alignment now, but any future program will need its own terms before they can judge what benefit it delivers.

#5157. XRP Ledger Activates New Amendment to Strengthen Network

The XRP Ledger (XRPL) network recently activated a new amendment that helps secure the network by resolving potential security weaknesses and making some existing functionalities more robust and reliable.

This latest amendment, called fixCleanup3_4_0, was activated on Nov. 4, bringing a series of technical updates that include fixes for token issuance, automated market makers (AMMs), lending functionalities, and other parts of the XRP Ledger network.

A fix for a potential security weak point related to token issuance on the XRP Ledger network is a critical aspect of the latest amendment.

As explained in the official documentation of the XRP Ledger network, this update addresses a potential weakness related to delegated permissions that could allow some accounts to issue tokens when they are only allowed to destroy them.

On the XRP Ledger, token issuers can grant permissions to other accounts to perform certain tasks. For example, a stablecoin issuer could delegate permission to burn tokens to another account when customers want to redeem them.

With the latest amendment, the XRP Ledger network addresses this security weakness by preventing a zero-balance, burn-only delegated payment from resulting in the issuance of new tokens.

On top of addressing token issuance permissions, there's more to the fixCleanup3_4_0 amendment.

As explained in the official XRP Ledger network documentation, the fixCleanup3_4_0 update brings several fixes to decentralized trading, asset management, and transaction processing.

For automated market makers (AMMs), this latest update prevents some invalid auction bids and makes some improvements to how liquidity withdrawals and token clawbacks work.

It also brings some fixes for potential unexpected calculation errors or rounding issues that could occur in some cases.

These changes are supposed to make things more predictable when it comes to liquidity pools and avoid potential failures that could happen in some cases.

Another significant aspect of these changes concerns Single Asset Vaults and the proposed native lending infrastructure on the XRP Ledger.

As explained, this amendment brings some improvements to how rounding is handled when assets enter or exit vaults.

The idea is to ensure that transactions do not result in more or fewer assets being transferred than they should.

#5156. Democrats to Target Trump’s Crypto Ventures After Taking Congress

Democrats are reportedly preparing subpoenas targeting President Trump's World Liberty Financial ties, its $500 million Emirati stake and the Changpeng Zhao pardon.

House Democrats plan to open investigations into President Donald Trump’s family crypto businesses once they take control in January, with World Liberty Financial, its Emirati investor and the pardon of Binance founder Changpeng Zhao at the top of the list.

Rep. Robert Garcia of California, who will chair the House Oversight Committee, answered the Associated Press call with a two-word statement at 3:18 a.m.: “Lawyer up.” His office said he intends to take on what it calls the “Trump Crime Family” alongside corporate corruption, immigration enforcement abuses and the Epstein files.

Garcia told Punchbowl News the committee will examine the family’s crypto deals, Donald Trump Jr.’s business dealings and relatives such as Jared Kushner “sitting on boards and getting contracts.”

The difference from the past two years is subpoena power. Democrats have written letters, requested Treasury probes and held shadow hearings; the majority lets them compel documents and testimony from the administration and from private companies without the president’s consent.

Reuters reported in September that inquiries could begin as early as February, with corporate donors to the White House ballroom and firms that did deals with Trump relatives among the first targets.

World Liberty Financial, the DeFi and stablecoin venture the Trump family launched in September 2024, is expected to come first. Democrats have focused on a $500 million investment by Sheikh Tahnoon bin Zayed Al Nahyan, the UAE national security adviser, whose vehicle took a reported 49% stake shortly before the January 2025 inauguration.

The stake matters because World Liberty is seeking a federal trust bank charter for its USD1 stablecoin; the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency granted conditional approval on Aug. 14, with final sign-off pending. Tahnoon’s group and a Trump-affiliated entity together control about 87% of the bank’s holding company, according to The Wall Street Journal.

House Democrats asked Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent in February to investigate the deal and sought suspicious activity reports tied to the family’s ventures. Those requests carried no legal force. Senate Democrats on the Permanent Subcommittee on Investigations, led by Richard Blumenthal, opened their own inquiry into the TRUMP token and World Liberty earlier this year.

The second target is the October 2025 pardon of Zhao, whose exchange helped launch USD1 and was hacked for $6.34 billion last week. Zhao has said he has no business ties to the Trump family and did not discuss clemency with them. Trump’s 2025 financial disclosure listed more than $1 billion in crypto-related income, including about $635 million in TRUMP memecoin royalties; Reuters put the family’s crypto earnings at $2.3 billion or more.

The investigations will not produce legislation. Trump has said he could become “Mr. Veto,” and Democrats lack the two-thirds majority in either chamber to override him. The White House dismissed the plans before the vote; spokeswoman Lauren Bis called them a “decade long witch hunt.”

Democrats expect the administration to invoke executive privilege and slow-walk requests, as it did in Trump’s first term, and plan to subpoena private entities tied to the family instead.

The damage for crypto is political rather than statutory. TD Cowen’s Jaret Seiberg wrote Wednesday that the investigations “may make it impossible to advance a version of Clarity that the crypto sector could back,” and Bloomberg Intelligence has flagged crypto, defense and technology as the sectors most exposed to probes.

#5155. Ripple Executive Reveals Massive Growth of Company's London Hub

According to Ripple's UK and Europe Managing Director, Cassie Craddock, Ripple’s London office has expanded significantly over the last decade to become Ripple’s largest international hub outside the U.S.

“When we moved into our current office in 2018, there were 2 floors, and a lot of empty desks. Now we’re on 3 floors and full on all the desks. We’re now over capacity,” she wrote on X.

Craddock's comments came after she took part in a fireside chat at the British Residence in New York as part of Ripple’s Swell conference.

She revealed that Ripple chose to focus on London early on, given the UK's strong capital markets, mature legal system, experienced regulators and concentration of financial talent.

However, she added that regulatory uncertainty had previously limited the company’s capacity to build more relationships with leading U.K. financial institutions.

Ripple's story in the U.K. capital began on March 21, 2016, when the San Francisco-based company officially announced the launch of its first office in London, located in Canary Wharf.

Back then, the expansion was meant to accommodate the growing interest of European banks in distributed ledger technology and cross-border settlement solutions.

Ripple incorporated its U.K. entity in April 2016 under the name Ripple Europe Ltd. (later changed to Ripple Markets UK Ltd. in August 2022).

Ripple continued to expand its ties to U.K. financial institutions in the following years.

In April 2018, Santander introduced its new international payments app, One Pay FX, which used Ripple’s xCurrent technology.

In February 2022, Ripple announced a partnership with London-based payments company Modulr to make it easier to send cross-border payments to the UK and Europe.

Moreover, Ripple has played a role in developing the research landscape for blockchain in the U.K. In January 2026, Ripple shared that it had pledged more than £5M to UK universities under its University Blockchain Research Initiative.

One of the major milestones for Ripple in the U.K. occurred on Jan. 9, when Ripple received an Electronic Money Institution license and cryptoasset registration from the Financial Conduct Authority. The authorizations enabled Ripple to extend its regulated cross-border payment services to UK institutions.

In February, Ripple also secured a partnership with Aviva Investors to explore the tokenization of traditional investment funds.

This work resulted in the launch of a tokenized share class of Aviva Investors’ £22bn Sterling Liquidity Fund on the XRP Ledger on Oct. 26.

#5154. Aave and Lido lose $1.7B in massive DeFi capital drop after the Binance hack

Binance's $6.34 billion hack strained DeFi lending as stablecoin supply shrank by over $10 billion and major protocols lost billions in TVL.

Binance's security breach hit a crypto market whose stablecoin market was already shrinking, as DeFiLlama's dashboard put stablecoin market capitalization at $293.8 billion by Nov. 3, down over $10 billion in 30 days.

Binance's Nov. 3 report estimated that approximately $6.34 billion in assets were impacted on October 29 and said the company would absorb the financial impact.

The exchange's Oct. 29 incident added a separate shock to decentralized lending when an exploiter-linked wallet supplied USDT to the Aave lending protocol.

Aave's risk service provider LlamaRisk reported that an exploiter-linked wallet supplied 60 million USDT and borrowed 19,762 units of Wrapped Ethereum (WETH), worth about $44.6 million at the time.

Its Oct. 30 assessment described the position as overcollateralized and subject to normal liquidation, and said it did not put suppliers at risk.

Guardians subsequently froze 175 Aave V3 reserves and 117 V4 reserves. New supply and borrowing stopped in frozen reserves, while repayments, withdrawals and liquidations remained enabled.

Suppliers could withdraw available tokens, but fresh deposits could not replace them. LlamaRisk said withdrawals pushed some large reserves to 92%-100% utilization, meaning almost all supplied funds were borrowed. Borrowing costs rose, and stewards temporarily reduced the upper part of the interest-rate curves.

The affected reserves had reopened by Oct. 31, and LlamaRisk reported on its Nov. 2 update the restoration of specified non-stablecoin collateral settings and said affected reserves had returned to pre-incident rate curves on Nov. 1.

USDT, USDC, and USDT0 still had zero collateral borrowing power in affected configurations.

Among the large protocols examined, Aave recorded the biggest dollar decline between Oct. 27 and Nov. 3. Its dated daily total value locked (TVL) fell by about $1 billion, and Lido followed with a $713 million decline.

Falling token prices can reduce TVL without depositors moving assets, so a smaller TVL does not by itself establish either capital flight or insolvency.

USDT and USDC accounted for about $8.4 billion of the implied 30-day market-cap decline, based on DeFiLlama's rounded values and percentage changes.

DeFiLlama's separate historical series, which has different coverage from the dashboard, fell from $308.8 billion on Oct. 4 to $303.3 billion on Oct. 27.

Issuer redemptions exchange circulating stablecoins for fiat, differing from moving tokens off an exchange or between wallets. Freezing stolen tokens also does not automatically remove them from supply.

The practical risk is the amount of liquidity available where they need it. Thinner trading pools can produce more slippage for the same trade, lending withdrawals can raise borrowing costs, and falling collateral prices increase liquidation exposure.

Conditions would look healthier with renewed stablecoin net issuance, lending deposits returning, lower reserve utilization with more withdrawable tokens, and steadier collateral prices. A rebound in dollar TVL alone would not show that lending cash had returned.

#5152. Librería de Satoshi Rebrands to ISMOS, Launches Global Platform to Train One Million Bitcoin Developers

Librería de Satoshi, the technical education organization, recently announced the launch of its global platform as they rebrand to ISMOS and shows off a new website. The organization aims to expand its Bitcoin and open-source developer education program throughout the world, creating local hubs across the world and delivering education programs in local languages.Since 2021, ISMOS has reached more than 3,665 people in more than 40 countries and has financially supported 30 open-source developers, according to a press release shared with Bitcoin Magazine.

“Talent is universal; opportunity is not. I founded Librería de Satoshi because I experienced the language barrier in Bitcoin firsthand. ISMOS takes that mission worldwide, so that the next great developer can come from anywhere,” said Dulce Villarreal, CEO and founder of ISMOS.

What began as a Bitcoin school for Spanish speakers is today a technical education ecosystem and a complete training path for freedom technologies and open-source developers. ISMOS brings together educators and technology creators: a space to learn and teach with “Freedom Tech values, in more languages, more regions, and more disciplines, with a growing presence in universities, bootcamps, and programming communities.” ISMOS was born in Latin America as Librería de Satoshi and is now a global movement.

One Million Bitcoin Developers Around the World

ISMOS combines rigorous study programs, direct mentorship, and fellowships in Spanish, Portuguese, and English, and soon in more languages. Subjects will include Bitcoin, cryptography, cybersecurity, mathematics, AI, Nostr, open-source software, and open AI models. The platform is open source, and software developers can explore its open tools at github.com/ismos-xyz.

Today ISMOS is primarily supported by Btrust and the Human Rights Foundation. Other organizations, such as OpenSats and the Gemini Opportunity Fund, have supported its work in the past with smaller grants, according to the press release.

The announcement reflects nearly five years of growth and a deeper commitment to equal opportunity. “Someone coding in Argentina, Nigeria, or El Salvador deserves the same chance to build a first-class technical career as anyone in Silicon Valley or London.”

ISMOS aims to go beyond study programs to provide individual support to the best developers through programs such as B4OS (Bitcoin 4 Open Source), fellowships, and its developer residencies: “in their language, at their level, and with the specific support they need to go further than they imagined.”

#5147. Jack Dorsey’s Block (XYZ) Sets Aside $764 Million for DOJ Settlement as Bitcoin Profit Halves

Jack Dorsey’s Block agreed in principle to a $764 million deferred-prosecution deal over anti-money-laundering failures at Cash App, a sum that exceeds its $566 million Q3 profit, while bitcoin gross profit fell 50% after the company cut trading fees.

Block has agreed in principle to pay $764 million to resolve a Justice Department investigation into its anti-money-laundering controls, a sum larger than the $566 million profit the company reported for the third quarter.

The agreement, reached in October and disclosed in a 10-Q filed Nov. 3, would defer for three years a criminal charge that Block failed to maintain an effective anti-money-laundering program under the Bank Secrecy Act.

The gross figure is about $964 million, reduced to $764 million after credits for earlier regulatory settlements. Block has accrued the full amount and said losses above it are “reasonably possible.”

The company disputes the basis and methodology of the government’s assessment, and the deal requires final approval.

The settlement overshadowed a quarter in which gross profit rose 22% to $3.24 billion, with Cash App up 27% to $2.07 billion and Square up 14% to $1.16 billion, according to the company release.

Bitcoin was the exception. Gross profit from the bitcoin business fell 50% to $52 million from $104 million a year earlier, and bitcoin revenue dropped 11% to $1.8 billion.

The investigation dates to March 2023, when Hindenburg Research alleged that Cash App inflated user counts with duplicate and fraudulent accounts and that weak identity checks let criminals use the platform for scams and money laundering. Block called the report inaccurate and misleading.

The Justice Department and SEC opened inquiries into the compliance practices and related disclosures; the SEC closed its side in March without recommending enforcement.

State regulators moved first. In April 2025 the New York Department of Financial Services fined Block $40 million after finding a backlog of suspicious-activity alerts that grew from about 18,000 in 2018 to more than 169,000 by 2020, and a screening system that did not block bitcoin transfers to wallets linked to terrorism financing until exposure passed 10%.

A multistate settlement in January 2025 added $80 million. Those payments account for part of the $200 million credit against the DOJ figure.

The deferred prosecution structure means Block avoids a conviction if it meets the agreement’s terms over three years, a path Binance took in 2023 at a cost of $4.3 billion.

The charge lands as Block pursues an industrial bank charter for a unit it calls Builders Bank, which would offer custody and fiduciary services for bitcoin. Bank regulators weigh compliance history in charter decisions.

The drop in bitcoin profit was a choice. Block said the decline came from its decision to cut fees on certain Cash App bitcoin transactions, part of a push to make the asset a payment rail rather than a trading product.

The company auto-enabled about 1 million Square merchants for Lightning payments in May, waived processing fees on bitcoin through 2026, and in October launched a national ad campaign built on spending bitcoin rather than holding it.

Revenue from bitcoin sales to customers, which Block books at gross, fell as the price dropped from about $114,000 to $70,000 over the year.

Block held 28,355 BTC across its treasury and customer balances as of the first quarter, including about 9,000 in the corporate treasury, and continues to put 10% of bitcoin gross profit into purchases.

#5144. Nvidia stock tokens show price spreads, but slippage crushes the arbitrage

Two $1,000 comparisons leave about 1 basis point and negative 15 before fees, while access and share adjustments complicate the trade.

Tokens tracking Nvidia shares can trade at different prices across issuers and exchanges, but buying the cheaper version and selling a dearer one leaves little room for profit in two modeled comparisons published by data provider Coin Metrics on Nov. 3.

The data provider's execution study shows about one basis point remaining in one $1,000 comparison after entry and exit slippage, and a negative 15 basis points in another.

The results show how a visible price difference can shrink when an order reaches the market, while preserving a small positive remainder in the first example.

A holder must compare the same share exposure, have something to sell on the expensive side, and qualify for the route used to settle the trade.

Coin Metrics' spread and slippage chart compares buying Hyperliquid NVDAx with selling Binance NVDAb, printing a gross gap of 10 basis points, entry and exit slippage of 10 basis points, and a one-basis-point remainder before trading fees.

#5141. Strategy’s STRC Hits $100 Par for First Time Since May After Over $2 Billion in Buybacks

Strategy’s STRC preferred hit $100 par on Wednesday for the first time since May, after billions in buybacks, a 12% dividend rate and a switch to daily payouts.

Strategy’s Stretch preferred stock touched $100 today for the first time since May, returning to the par value the company has spent over $2 billion in buybacks and five months of rate increases to defend.

STRC traded below $80 at times in June, a massive discount to its stated value. The climb back to $100 restores the price at which Strategy says it will resume issuing new shares, the mechanism that funds its bitcoin purchases. Chairman Michael Saylor had pledged to keep the stock at or above par and to hold the dividend at 12% until it got there.

Two changes landed in the past week. Shareholders approved daily dividends on STRC and three other preferreds on Oct. 28 by a 99.3% vote, removing the semi-monthly ex-dividend drops that had added volatility to the price.

The first daily payment went out Monday. And Strategy’s Oct. 29 filing set the November rate at 12% annualized, or $1 per share for the month.

Shares of Strategy (MSTR) were up over 7% today after bitcoin jumped above $80,000.

BREAKING: Michael Saylor's STRC has returned to par for the first time since May! 👀They will start buying more bitcoin as soon as it goes above $100 🚀 pic.twitter.com/niNmngTP1j

Stretch, the Variable Rate Series A Perpetual Stretch Preferred, launched in July 2025 at $90 with a 9% starting rate. Saylor called it the company’s “iPhone moment.” The design is a feedback loop: Strategy adjusts the dividend each month to hold the price near $100, sells new shares when it trades above par and uses the cash to buy bitcoin.

It became the largest tradable preferred in the world, with a market value above $8 billion and a holder base that CEO Phong Le has put at about 80% retail.

The loop broke earlier this year. STRC drifted below par in late January; Strategy raised the rate to 11.25% in February, 11.5% in March and 12% by summer, and the price kept sliding as bitcoin fell toward $65,000. At $89 the at-the-market program sat idle, cutting off the company’s cheapest funding.

In late May, Strategy sold 32 BTC for about $2.5 million to cover a Stretch distribution, its first bitcoin sale since 2022, and critics raised the prospect of a “death spiral” that analysts at Benchmark and TD Cowen disputed.

The fix was to buy the stock back. Strategy paused bitcoin purchases in the week of Oct. 19 and spent $204.9 million on STRC alone, part of the roughly $2.25 billion it has retired since the discount opened.

Each share bought below $100 shrank the float and reduced the dividend bill at the same time.

The recovery matters because of what sits beneath it. Strategy holds 849,327 BTC after adding 811 coins last week, at an average cost of about $75,446. With bitcoin near $78,300 on Wednesday, the position is about 4% above water.

The company reported $20.91 billion in third-quarter operating income, almost all of it unrealized gains, alongside a negative year-to-date BTC yield and a chief financial officer stepping down.

The preferred stack is the bill that comes due regardless. STRC, STRF, STRK and STRD together carry annual dividends in the hundreds of millions, and the quarter’s 5,552 BTC in sales went to fund them and the buybacks. A Stretch at par lets Strategy pay those obligations with new preferred issuance rather than coins. A Stretch at $89 does not.

The test now is whether the company reopens the ATM at $100 and whether buyers absorb it.

#5136. Matt Corallo Flags Critical Linux Kernel Flaw; Lightning Labs Ships LND Mitigation

Bitcoin Core and Lightning developer Matt Corallo highlighted a severe Linux kernel vulnerability on October 30, criticizing Ubuntu’s delayed response to what he called “the most critical Linux vuln ever.” The bug, tracked as CVE-2026-98432, affects the kernel’s IPv4 TCP handshake code with a CVSS severity score of 9.8. Anthropic’s Frontier Red Team later showed it could be turned into a remote root exploit against systems exposing a Multipath TCP (MPTCP) listener.

Peter Todd echoed the warning the same day, advising anyone running LND on Linux with open ports to shut their Bitcoin Lightning nodes down until the kernel was patched or otherwise mitigated. The demonstrated exploit path relies on an MPTCP listener—something Go 1.24 and later enable by default. Because LND is written in Go, recent versions were listening with MPTCP on their peer, gRPC, and REST ports whenever the kernel had the feature enabled.

Lightning Labs responded the same day with emergency releases: lnd v0.21.6-beta and the backport v0.20.7-beta. Both disable MPTCP listeners by default, closing the demonstrated exploit path through LND. The project described the change as a mitigation, not a fix, and noted that only a patched kernel fully resolves the underlying issue. Other services on the same machine remain exposed until MPTCP is disabled host-wide or the kernel is updated. Olaoluwa Osuntokun and the official Lightning Labs account both recommended the update.

Security researcher Marcus Hutchins later reported internet-wide scanning activity consistent with threat actors looking for exposed systems. His scans found roughly 2.1 million servers reachable over MPTCP on port 443. International Cyber Digest amplified the finding on November 4, noting the scanning and Ubuntu’s slower patch timeline.

Canonical has since made fixed kernels available in the opt-in, proposed pocket for Ubuntu 22.04, 24.04, and 26.04, but they have not yet reached the regular updates channel. Red Hat shipped fixes for RHEL 8/9/10 on November 1; Debian, Amazon Linux, and others had already released patches earlier.

UmbrelOS 2.0.1 included the kernel fix on November 1st for x86 devices and applied a traffic-blocking mitigation on Raspberry Pi, where a fixed kernel was not yet available. No confirmed in-the-wild exploitation has been reported, and the vulnerability has not been added to CISA’s Known Exploited Vulnerabilities catalog. Anthropic’s remote-root demonstration remains unpublished.

The practical exposure is highest on internet-facing servers. Home desktop systems behind typical NAT and firewalls, without public TCP listeners, are far less affected. For Lightning operators on Linux, the LND releases remove the specific MPTCP listener path the public exploit used, but the broader kernel bug remains until a patched kernel is installed.

#5135. Ethereum Layer 2 Aztec gets Tierra V6 upgrade to support faster private apps

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Tierra V6 fixes a critical V5 security issue and speeds up private transaction proofs, with new apps launching or preparing to launch on Aztec.

The upgrade resolves vulnerabilities found in earlier versions, including a critical issue in V5, the foundation said. It also adds a new AI-based system for security testing.

When asked why the critical issue wasn't fixed sooner, an Aztec spokesperson said the fix required the whole network to move to a new version.

"Because Aztec is a decentralized network, if a critical vulnerability is discovered, the entire network must upgrade to the new version to implement the fix," the spokesperson told The Block.

Aztec has no central operator that can make the change on its own, the spokesperson said, adding that there are plans to make future upgrades faster if vulnerabilities are discovered.

Aztec said its network has more than 3,000 validators, no centralized sequencer, and no privileged viewing keys.

Besides the security fixes, Tierra makes private transaction proofs twice as fast, taking one second to generate on a native device, according to the foundation.

Messages from Ethereum can now reach Aztec in as little as 12 seconds, compared with an average of two minutes before, the foundation said, noting that the change speeds up deposits and other transactions between the networks.

Developers on Aztec can choose which functions of their apps are private and which are public, the foundation said, adding that private functions run and generate proofs on the user's device, while public functions run on the network.

The upgrade comes shortly after Aztec Labs relaunched zk.money, a self-custodial wallet for private crypto payments, three years after discontinuing the original product.

"With Aztec Tierra and zk.money, users can deposit directly from any Ethereum wallet and make lightning-fast private transactions without complicated bridging workflows," Joe Andrews, co-founder and CEO of Aztec Labs, said in a statement.

Other apps launching or preparing to launch include Galactica for private AI inference, Nemi for private perpetuals trading, and the private browser wallet Nulo, according to the announcement.

Xochi supports private intents and trading, while Nyx lets users make private payments to any Ethereum wallet. Olla offers liquid staking for the Aztec token.

Rolling Chat, a private message board based on Ethereum co-founder Vitalik Buterin's Aztec experiments, is also on the list.

Aztec also introduced a new brand and website alongside Tierra, describing its aim as becoming the platform for "unstoppable private apps."

#5134. Bitcoin Firm Strive Climbs Up the Treasury Ranks With Another Big Buy

Bitcoin Firm Strive Climbs Up the Treasury Ranks With Another Big Buy

Strive, the Dallas-based company co-founded by Vivek Ramaswamy, now holds 43,124 Bitcoin after buying 124 BTC more last week. That stash is now worth roughly $3.3 billion, and good for about 0.2% of the 21 million coins that will ever exist.

That leaves it fourth among publicly traded companies, 390 coins behind Twenty One Capital (which holds 43,514 BTC) and 876 BTC behind Metaplanet (which holds 44,000 BTC), according to Bitcoin Treasuries data.

At Bitcoin’s price of about $77,000, those gaps are worth roughly $30 million and $67 million, respectively.

Strive is a Bitcoin treasury company—a publicly listed firm that keeps Bitcoin on its balance sheet, so anyone with a brokerage account can get exposure by buying its stock. Michael Saylor’s Strategy popularized the playbook back in 2020, and several other companies followed suit, including Strive.

Its coins cost an average of $87,785 each, or $3.8 billion in total, against a market value of $3.5 billion on Oct. 23, per its prospectus. Bitcoin has slipped since, so the pile is now worth a bit less than Strive paid for it.

The latest purchase covers Oct. 24 through Oct. 30 and was disclosed today in an SEC filing, the report US-listed companies must submit when something material happens. Strive paid about $10 million, an average of $80,526 per Bitcoin including fees, CEO Matt Cole said on X.

Strive acquired 124 $BTC for $10M at an average cost of $80,526 per bitcoin, bringing total holdings to ₿43,124.⁰⁰- Existing cash funded most purchases⁰- SATS ATM used for first time, representing 100% of weekly ATM proceeds⁰- Amplification Ratio: 62.7%⁰⁰$ASST $SATA $SATS pic.twitter.com/rFrS6y4AVc

— Matt Cole (@ColeMacro) November 2, 2026

Most of the money came from existing cash, which fell from $504.4 million to $490.4 million over the week. Strive also raised money for the first time by selling shares of a new security called SATS on the open market, Cole said, and that made up all of the week’s fundraising. It still holds 505,000 shares of Strategy’s preferred stock, known as STRC, worth about $50 million.

SATS is a preferred stock—a type of share that pays a fixed dividend before common shareholders see anything. Strive sold 7 million of them at $90 each in an October offering that raised about $630 million, and they carry a 15% annual dividend on a $100 stated value, paid every business day from Nov. 9.

On Oct. 26, Strive opened an at-the-market program—a way to sell new shares in small batches at the going price—for up to $1 billion more SATS. The prospectus says Strive expects to pay the dividends mainly by raising more capital, including selling more shares. The company has set aside 18 months of dividends in a reserve and reported no debt as of Oct. 23.

Strive’s amplification ratio, the preferred stock and debt it carries measured against the value of its Bitcoin, stood at 62.7%, or about $62.70 of obligations for every $100 of Bitcoin.

The company’s CEO has said ending 2026 as the second-largest public Bitcoin holder is possible. “I wouldn’t say that’s my base case,” Cole said on the One Chair podcast in September, “but I would say it’s in the possible set of cards.”

Saylor’s Strategy holds 849,327 Bitcoin, nearly 20 times Strive’s pile.

Twenty One Capital still holds the same 43,514 Bitcoin it had when co-founder Jack Mallers stepped down as CEO in July. His exit came as a plan by Tether to merge three Bitcoin businesses collapsed. The stock had fallen more than 80% from its highs, and his successor, Raphael Zagury, has said Twenty One should be judged on cash flow and capital discipline.

Metaplanet, a Tokyo-listed company that pivoted from hotels to Bitcoin in 2024, holds 44,000 Bitcoin after overtaking mining firm MARA in April. It has a stated goal of 100,000 by the end of 2026.

At last week’s pace of 124 Bitcoin, Strive would pass Twenty One in four weeks and Metaplanet in eight, assuming neither company buys more.

#5131. Bitcoin Price Holds Near $80,000 as Democratic Sweep Sparks Short Squeeze

Bitcoin price holds near $80,000 after rebounding from an election-night low of $75,600, as a short squeeze, a stock rally and a 9% drop in oil follow the Democratic sweep of Congress.

Bitcoin price traded near $80,000 on Wednesday afternoon, up about 3% on the day and roughly $26,000 below its price one year ago, as markets shook off election-night jitters over the Democratic sweep of Congress, according to Bitcoin Magazine Pro data.

Bitcoin changed hands at $80,099 at 3:12 p.m. ET, with a market cap of about $1.61 trillion and roughly $39.6 billion in trading volume over the past 24 hours.

The low came in the 10 p.m. ET hour on Tuesday, as results showed Democrats taking both chambers. The high came in the 10 a.m. hour on Wednesday. That is a swing of about 7.3% from bottom to top in roughly 12 hours.

The climb was a short squeeze. About $950 million in leveraged crypto positions were liquidated over 24 hours, and bets against prices made up $560 million of that. Bitcoin positions accounted for $403 million of the total, 76% of it shorts.

CryptoQuant CEO Ki Young Ju wrote on X Wednesday afternoon that $215 million in bitcoin shorts were liquidated in a single hour.

JUST IN: Bitcoin surges to $81,000 🚀 pic.twitter.com/k1bGzrrzCP

The view among analysts is that the sell-off had already done its work. CoinShares head of research James Butterfill said the sweep was largely priced into bitcoin before the vote, leaving room for a relief rally once the uncertainty cleared.

Lumida CEO Ram Ahluwalia had laid out the same path in a podcast interview the day before the election. A Democratic sweep, he said, would bring “a one and done washout, and that resets where asset prices are.”

Bitcoin price did not rally alone. The Dow Jones Industrial Average rose about 1,500 points, or 3%, on Wednesday afternoon, while the S&P 500 climbed 1.7% and the Nasdaq Composite gained 1.8%. Apple and Microsoft touched record highs.

Oil moved the most. Brent crude fell more than 9% to $93.91 a barrel and West Texas Intermediate dropped nearly 9% to $87.59 by 12:36 p.m. ET, CNBC reported, after Vice President JD Vance blamed the Republican losses on the Iran war.

“The reason we lost is the war,” Vance wrote on X.

That matters for bitcoin price because crude above $100 has kept inflation hot and the Federal Reserve leaning toward a December hike. The U.S. dollar index fell 0.55% to 101.32 in early trading, its lowest since Oct. 1.

Demand from funds has not caught up with the price. U.S. spot bitcoin ETFs shed $115.6 million on Tuesday, their third straight session of outflows, with BlackRock’s IBIT losing $95 million. The three-day total comes to about $1.16 billion. Wednesday’s flows will be reported after the U.S. close.

The policy picture got worse for the industry, not better. TD Cowen’s Jaret Seiberg wrote that the sweep makes a crypto-backed CLARITY Act “impossible,” and the committees that oversee crypto pass to Democrats in January.

On-chain analysts frame the move as a retest. Glassnode said in its Week on Chain report that the bitcoin price is testing the levels it broke above in August, with the True Market Mean at $77,700 and the short-term holder cost basis at $75,700. Holding above them, the firm wrote, keeps the August uptrend in place.

The calendar stays busy. The October jobs report lands Friday, October CPI follows on Nov. 10 and the Fed’s next decision comes on Dec. 9, as noted in Tuesday’s preview. Bitcoin price remains about 36% below its all-time high near $126,080.

The test now is whether ETF buyers return to carry the price once the shorts are gone. At the time of writing, the bitcoin price is near $80,200.

#5127. Binance says hackers used fake job interview, internal AI tool in $6.3 billion breach

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Binance said it detected and prevented 57 suspected North Korea-linked cyber threats in 2026, including similar attempts involving fraudulent interviews and malicious software.

Binance said hackers used a fraudulent job interview to compromise an employee’s computer, then used an internal AI tool to access its wallet infrastructure and steal more than $6.3 billion in digital assets.

In an initial incident report published Tuesday, the world's largest crypto exchange detailed how attackers used malware and stolen credentials to access its wallet-signing systems. Binance said it has frozen or intercepted about $2.85 billion in assets and replenished 98% of impacted assets.

"This wasn't one mistake or one breach — it was a months-long campaign. We're publishing the full report because the lessons go far beyond Binance," co-CEO Richard Teng said on X. "The speed of coordination could not be matched in traditional finance."

Binance said the FBI had linked the attack to North Korea, while its own investigation found that hackers used social engineering, malware, and compromised internal AI tooling to gain access to its wallet-signing infrastructure and authorize unauthorized transactions.

According to the report, the attackers first targeted a Wallet DevOps engineer in June through a tampered Homebrew installation downloaded while he was trying to install an AI coding tool. Binance said its security team detected the anomaly, replaced the device, and rotated the affected credentials, but the incident exposed information about the engineer's role and colleagues. In September, attackers then targeted another employee through a fraudulent recruitment process, posing as company executives before tricking him into downloading a malicious coding assignment that infected his work computer and gave hackers unauthorized access.

On Oct. 20, the attackers used the compromised employee's computer and an internal AI tool to gain control of two other engineers' devices, discovering a container deployment permission on one of them. Nine days later, they used the stolen credentials to deploy a container and run a malicious script that launched the wallet attack.

Binance said it detected and prevented 57 suspected North Korea-linked cyberattacks throughout 2026, many involving similar tactics such as fraudulent interviews, malicious developer tools, and social engineering. On Monday, North Korean leader Kim Jong-un's sister called FBI claims linking North Korea to the hack a "grave political provocation."

Binance said it has since closed the entry points used in the attack, rebuilt or replaced affected systems, strengthened device controls, and expanded AI-based behavioral anomaly detection.

Separately, the exchange continued restoring normal operations, reopening withdrawals in phases for dozens of assets across BSC, Ethereum, Solana, XRP, Sui, Arbitrum, Optimism, TRON and Base networks starting at 4 a.m. UTC on Nov. 3. Binance said it was prioritizing tokens based on user demand over the past week. The exchange later reported that hourly deposits exceeded withdrawals, describing the shift as a sign that flows were stabilizing and user activity was improving.

#5126. House Democrats Eye Their Own Crypto Market Structure Bill With a Bigger SEC Role After Sweep

House Democrats are discussing a plan to turn Rep. Maxine Waters’ Crypto Investor and Consumer Bill of Rights into their own crypto market structure bill next Congress, with ideas that include a bigger role for the SEC and a smaller one for the CFTC, Eleanor Terrett reported Wednesday.

The proposal would fold in the Democratic Clarity Act provisions Waters offered in the House, which were not adopted, plus the provisions Senate Democrats were able to agree on with Republicans before September’s failed cloture vote. Terrett cited three industry sources who have spoken with Democratic staffers.

Further ethics provisions restricting the crypto activities of President Donald Trump and other federal officials are also likely, she wrote. Republicans are calling it a “messaging bill.”

No text for the crypto market structure bill has been released, and the details so far come from industry sources rather than from lawmakers.

JUST IN: 🇺🇸 House Democrats reportedly want to revive their Clarity Act provisions in a broader crypto market structure bill next Congress, Eleanor Terrett reports. 👀 pic.twitter.com/8twokppjHz

The talks follow Tuesday’s Democratic sweep of Congress. Waters, the top Democrat on the House Financial Services Committee, is in line to take the gavel in January from Chairman French Hill, the Clarity Act’s sponsor.

Bloomberg Law reported Wednesday that the new majorities give Democrats leverage over the Clarity Act, the industry’s top legislative priority, starting with tougher ethics rules aimed at Trump’s ventures.

Waters offered the bill of rights as amendment #31 to H.R. 3633 in July 2025. The House Rules Committee blocked it 4-8 along party lines, on a motion from Rep. Mary Gay Scanlon of Pennsylvania. Making an amendment “in order” lets the full House debate and vote on it, so the result kept the package off the floor.

The committee’s record lists what it would have required: 24/7 customer service, insurance or SIPC-like coverage, fiduciary and best-execution rules, anti-front-running rules, bankruptcy-remote custody with 24-hour withdrawals and monthly auditor-attested proof of reserves. It would also have sent more funding to the SEC and CFTC.

The committee rejected a second Democratic amendment by the same 4-8 margin. That one, from Rep. Joe Neguse of Colorado, would have barred members of Congress, presidents and vice presidents from issuing or endorsing digital assets. The ethics fight it previewed later followed the bill to the Senate.

The agency split is where a Democratic crypto market structure bill would break most sharply from the Republican one. The House-passed Clarity Act directs the CFTC to regulate most digital commodity trading, including exchanges, brokers and dealers, and exempts tokens on mature blockchains from SEC registration under certain conditions.

That design is the one the industry backed. A crypto market structure bill that moves supervision back toward the SEC would reverse it.

The Clarity Act passed the House 294-134 in July 2025 and failed a Senate cloture vote 49-50 on Sept. 15. Sen. Thom Tillis entered a motion to reconsider, which preserves the option of another vote, but the bill dies when this Congress ends on Jan. 3 unless the lame-duck session revives it.

Democrats now hold the leverage. “The industry and the Republicans screwed up. That was the best bill they were going to get,” Sen. Ruben Gallego of Arizona, a Senate Banking Committee member, said in comments reported by Bloomberg Law.

TD Cowen’s Jaret Seiberg wrote Wednesday that the sweep closes the door on Clarity for two years, and that Democratic investigations of the Trump family’s crypto ventures “may make it impossible to advance a version of Clarity that the crypto sector could back.”

A Democratic crypto market structure bill would still need Trump’s signature to become law. Whether one built around the SEC can draw enough Republican votes to get there, or remains the messaging bill Republicans say it is, is the question the next Congress opens with.

#5125. Claude Fable 5.5, GPT 6.2 Astra Could Be Right Around the Corner, Leaks Suggest

Claude Fable 5.5, GPT 6.2 Astra Could Be Right Around the Corner, Leaks Suggest

Users on X say Anthropic is quietly testing an unreleased version of Mythos and Fable, and OpenAI is about to launch GPT 6.2 Astra.

Nobody has confirmed anything but the rumors are based on a handful of showcase demos, plus a catalog entry and a hidden website label that were both removed soon after appearing.

Fable is the top commercial tier of Claude, the chatbot that competes with ChatGPT, and the priciest flagship Anthropic sells for writing software and handling office work. Mythos is a restricted model that Anthropic offers to a select group of clients, including government and cyber giants due to its capabilities.

Anthropic filed for an IPO, its first sale of shares to the public, on October 26, reporting $16.29 billion in revenue for the first half of 2026 against $1.16 billion a year earlier.

Anthropic launched Fable 5 on June 9, pulled it offline for 18 days under U.S. export controls, which are government limits on who may use a technology, and released Fable 5.1 on September 1. Opus 5.5 followed on September 22, and Anthropic says it performs at the level of Fable 5.1 on most tasks at a fraction of the price.

The chatter began on October 1, when users posted screenshots of Claude still labeled Fable 5.1 beside outputs they said looked stronger. On October 11, pseudonymous user Chetaslua, known for sharing insights on unreleased models and spotting changes in outputs, logs and other signals of new models before they are officially announced, wrote that “Claude Opus 5.5 is routing to a new model,” adding that it could be either Fable 5.5 or a new Opus model.

#5122. CFTC approves individual stock perpetual futures trading in US for the first time

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Perpetual futures contracts surged in popularity over the past year, particularly after disruptions in global supply chains in the Middle East earlier this year at the onset of the war.

The Commodity Futures Trading Commission approved the ability for exchanges in the United States to offer perpetual futures contracts tied to individual stocks — just months after those assets were put into the spotlight by President Donald Trump.

On Tuesday, CFTC Chair Michael Selig greenlit the ability to trade those perpetual futures contracts for the first time in the U.S. for platforms.

"This expansion marks a significant step towards allowing U.S. market participants, consistent with applicable law, to avail themselves of the advantages of perpetual futures that have led to their emergence as widely sought tools for risk-management and price-discovery," Selig said.

Perpetuals, or perps, are a type of futures contract that don't have an expiration date and allow people to bet on the price movement of assets without owning them directly.

Perpetual futures contracts surged in popularity over the past year, particularly after disruptions in global supply chains in the Middle East earlier this year at the onset of the war, after the U.S. and Israel conducted missile strikes on Iran, which triggered supply shocks. That happened over the weekend, so traditional oil futures markets were closed, but platforms like Hyperliquid operate 24/7, so much of the trading happened onchain.

Over the past year, the CFTC has approved a few perpetual futures contracts. The agency first opened the door for bitcoin perpetual futures contracts in the U.S. when its staff greenlit KalshiEX and Coinbase to list the products. Then in June, the CFTC released a request for comment on crude oil perpetual contracts and 24/7 trading.

Perpetuals futures contracts have also caught Trump's attention. In August, he said he wanted the CFTC to work to bring Hyperliquid into the U.S.

Both the SEC and CFTC would oversee the contracts, Selig said on Tuesday.

"This framework recognized the complementary roles of the CFTC and SEC in overseeing American financial markets by providing a pathway for innovation at the intersection of the U.S.," Selig said.

#5121. French Bank Credit Mutuel Arkea Confirms Cyberattack as ZachXBT Puts Losses Above $133 Million

French bank Credit Mutuel Arkea said Wednesday that a cyberattack allowed hackers to carry out fraudulent transfers from the bank’s own account, a theft that on-chain investigator ZachXBT has put at more than $133 million and that the bank has not confirmed.

The Brittany-based lender said in a statement that the incident was under control, that operations were resuming and that customer assets were not affected. It suspended some transactions as a precaution.

“This incident has had no impact on (customer) assets and has only caused a delay in the execution of certain transfers,” the bank said on its website, in a translation published by Reuters.

In a follow-up question-and-answer note, Credit Mutuel Arkea said the attackers went through the platform of one of its service providers and reached the bank directly. They used the bank’s own account to send transactions to other banks, which then potentially allowed them to convert the money into cryptocurrency through specialized platforms, the bank said.

No customer accounts at its federations or subsidiaries were used, the bank said. It denied French press reports that the attackers got in through Fortuneo, its online banking arm, and said the Credit Mutuel Arkea hack was “a sophisticated attack from the outside,” not the result of human error.

The bank has not given a final figure. Asked whether 118 million euros, or $133 million, had been stolen, it said it does not confirm the number and can only “validate the order of magnitude.”

Part of the funds involved has been frozen and other steps are underway to recover the rest, the bank said. It added that the amounts at stake are “absolutely not” large enough to threaten its financial health or soundness.

AFP reported that the bank put the attack at about 100 million euros in a provisional estimate. Earlier Wednesday, Reuters reported that the bank said its losses were lower than $133 million. “It is still too early to give a final figure,” a spokesperson told the wire service.

ZachXBT first flagged the incident on Monday without naming the victim. He wrote on his Telegram channel that an EU entity was suspected of losing more than $106 million from multiple centralized exchange accounts on Nov. 1, with the funds withdrawn as USDC and USDT. He said the funds were then swapped for ether and moved across several chains.

In an update, he said he had found another $27 million or more in exchange withdrawals, bringing the total to more than $133 million. A later post named Credit Mutuel Arkea as the alleged victim. He wrote that the attack “occurred for multiple days” and that he suspects the amount stolen is larger once over-the-counter activity is counted.

The bank has not confirmed his figure or his timeline. It has also not said when the attack began, who was behind it or whether any data was taken. Credit Mutuel Arkea is working with judicial and police authorities to determine the origin of the attack and how it was carried out. It said it informed its supervisors once the attack was detected.

The bank also warned customers that phishing attempts are possible after the Credit Mutuel Arkea hack.

Credit Mutuel Arkea, which groups the Credit Mutuel federations of Brittany and the Southwest, is part of the wider Credit Mutuel group. It has 5.8 million clients and 211.5 billion euros, or about $238 billion, in assets, Reuters reported. It posted net profit of 230 million euros in the first half of the year, AFP reported.

The Credit Mutuel Arkea hack lands less than a week after the record $6.34 billion Binance hack, which blockchain analysis firms have linked to North Korea. ZachXBT said in his first post that he was highly confident this incident was not the work of North Korea’s Lazarus Group. Brazil’s Mercado Bitcoin also froze deposits and withdrawals this week after a security incident.

How much of the money the bank can recover, and what the final loss from the Credit Mutuel Arkea hack will be, remains open.
