#5029. French Hill’s race still too close to call as House flips, changing the direction of crypto legislation

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Rep. Hill has led the House Financial Services Committee for the past two years, during which members passed the first federal framework for stablecoins as well as a sweeping crypto bill.

As of Wednesday afternoon, House Financial Services Committee Chair French Hill's race for reelection is too close to call as a Democratic takeover in the House is set to usher in a new slate of leaders and reshape the future of crypto legislation in Congress.

On Tuesday night into Wednesday, Hill has been neck and neck with Democratic candidate Chris Jones in Arkansas, according to the Associated Press, with 99% of votes already reported. Hill held a slim lead at publication time.

Hill has led the House Financial Services Committee for the past two years, during which members passed the first federal framework for stablecoins as well as a sweeping crypto bill. The latter stalled in the Senate in September. Crypto political action committee Fairshake and other PACs spent $1 million on Hill's campaign, according to reporting from the Arkansas Democrat Gazette.

Other crypto-friendly lawmakers also secured reelection, including Josh Gottheimer, D-N.J., Rep. Warren Davidson, R-Ohio, Rep. Ritchie Torres, D-N.Y., and current House Agriculture Committee Chair Glenn "GT" Thompson, R-Pa. Later in the day on Wednesday, Rep. Bill Huizenga, R-MI., who is viewed favorably by the crypto industry, lost his seat to Democratic candidate Sean McCann, according to the Associated Press.

Candidate Shomari Figures beat Republican candidate Rhett Marques in Alabama. Figures was endorsed by crypto advocacy group Stand With Crypto, given his vote on sweeping digital asset legislation called the Clarity Act.

With the House flipping control, Democrats will usher in the agenda for key congressional committees that have jurisdiction over crypto and prediction markets. Top Democrat Maxine Waters will likely take back her spot as House Financial Services Committee chair, leaving Hill out of his top spot, but likely to be the top Republican on that committee.

Waters is still expected to take up the spot again as chair, but has since been challenged by Democratic Reps. Sean Casten and Bill Foster. Some Democrats have signaled that they want "generational change," given that Waters is 88 years old and Casten is 54, according to reporting from Politico last month.

Regarding crypto, Waters worked on the stablecoin framework dubbed the GENIUS Act and has shown an openness to writing rules for the industry. However, she has also criticized the industry following the collapse of crypto exchange FTX and, later, President Donald Trump's crypto interests and how that can influence regulation.

Fleece is a soft, synthetic fabric usually made from a polyester blend, often mixed with spandex, nylon, or cotton. Designed to mimic wool's insulating properties, fleece is lighter, more breathable, and dries faster, making it ideal for staying warm. Plus, fleece wicks away moisture and dries quickly, keeping you cozy even if you’re caught in rain or snow. Think of it like wearing a warm hug on a cold day.

When it comes to fleece leggings, the fabric blend is key. Most leggings are made from a mix of polyester and spandex, which delivers the perfect blend of warmth, stretch, and comfort. We’ll give you a quick breakdown of why these materials are key here:

Polyester traps heat, making it an ideal blend for staying warm without bulk. Compared to natural fibers like cotton, polyester dries quickly. WH editors love wearing polyester-fleece leggings on long walks since they stay warm without making you feel sweaty. It’s super lightweight yet strong, meaning you won’t feel like the material is clinging to your body. Polyester also helps leggings retain their shape over time, so stretching, sagging, and pilling are likely a thing of the past.

Nylon is a popular material in fleece leggings because of its strength, flexibility, and smooth texture. It resists wear and tear, which helps keep leggings looking brand new after every wash. Nylon also offers water resistance, which is key for outdoor activities in light rain or snow.

Spandex (also known as elastane or Lycra) is the go-to fabric for stretch and flexibility. It fits snugly and moves with you, giving you maximum comfort during high-impact activities like yoga, running, and dance. While it’s not as durable on its own, spandex is often blended with other fabrics (like nylon or polyester) to create leggings that are both stretchy and long-lasting.

Here’s the thing: Fleece leggings with thicker fabric may feel more restrictive than traditional leggings, so if you plan to wear them for high-impact activities, opting for a bigger size might be best. Look for four-way stretch fabrics like spandex for flexibility and comfort. If you're between sizes, choose based on your desired fit—size down for a sculpted look (read: tighter) or up for a relaxed, lounge-friendly feel.

Fleece leggings are perfect for workouts, offering warmth and insulation while keeping the stretch and comfort you need for full range of motion. “The leggings should have a good amount of stretch while fitting snugly, and maintaining their shape throughout the day,” says fashion designer Kadeem Alphanso Fyffe. Look for features like a wide, high-rise waistband for a secure fit.

We analyzed everything from fabric quality and warmth to stretch, fit, and breathability. Were they soft and supportive? Did they hold their shape after multiple washes? Could they withstand the elements without pilling or wearing thin? We examined every seam, ensuring each recommendation is a worthy investment for your winter wardrobe and beyond.
